Scott McTominay given police escort as drama 36 hours before Scotland World Cup clash laid bare

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Scott McTominay has revealed he had to have a police escort to a hospital in Boston to have his heart ‘shocked’ 36 hours before Scotland’s opening World Cup game against Haiti.

At the time, his absence from training and travel arrangements to Boston – he travelled from the team’s base in Charlotte separately from the rest of the team – were attributed to a stomach bug. In fact, it was a heart condition known as atrial fibrillation.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

“You're not going to say I have something to do with my heart two days before the first game of the World Cup,” he said.

“It's not an ideal scenario being in the hospital two days before—well, technically 36 hours before the first game of the World Cup. But you have to take the cards that you're dealt on the chin. It's part of the game. There's nothing I could do about it.”

Some will question the decision to let him play in not only the match against Haiti, which Scotland won 1-0, but also the two following group games against Morocco and Brazil. The Scots lost both fixtures and exited the World Cup with McTominay perceived to have been under par.

“It was in my hands,” he said. “The medical team reassured me it was safe to play and they said: ‘What do you think?' I said: ‘Let’s go for it’.”

Unbeknown to nearly everyone, and while Scotland fans were drinking bars dry in Boston, McTominay was at the centre of incredible drama.

Manager Steve Clarke told reporters that the midfielder had been laid low by a stomach bug. The reality of the situation - and the fact it was kept secret for so long - is barely believable given the global attention on the tournament and that McTominay is Scotland's star player.

He himself sought to downplay the situation on Tuesday at Hampden as he revealed the true story for the first time. “We got a police escort to go and get the shock then that was it to be honest,” recalled McTominay. “After that, I was back in normal rhythm, which is how a healthy heart should be then I went and played the game two days later.”

He described being put under anaesthetic. “You have to get put to sleep,” he said. “Whenever you go under anaesthetic and you have to get put to sleep, it decreases the athletic performance a little bit and (affects) sleep and stuff like that. It was far from ideal but those are the cards that you're dealt, there's nothing you can do about it.”

Although he stressed his family were fully on board with him playing, he wouldn’t have listened had it been otherwise. “All my family and friends had just flown 10 hours for the first game of the World Cup,” he said.

“I didn’t sit there thinking I might not play. That would never be the case. I was always going to play in that game. Maybe selfishly, I don’t know but that’s football. Players play with injuries all the time when they’re not quite right and it might be to the detriment of the team. (But) I was fully functional.”

The Napoli midfielder has since undergone an operation after the problem resurfaced following an early season Serie A game against Como. He returned to Manchester, where he had a procedure known as pulsed field ablation.

The player was sidelined for several weeks, missing the first games under new Scotland manager Sebastien Pocognoli but has returned to the squad for upcoming Nations League games against North Macedonia and Switzerland.

Zelda: Ocarina Of Time Remake Water Temple Walkthrough

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

Nintendo Switch 2The Legend of ZeldaThe Legend of Zelda: Ocarina Of Time

Well, you’ve made it. You’re finally at the most infamous dungeon in all of Zelda-dom, the Water Temple of Ocarina of Time. Good news for you: In the remake, this dungeon is far, far easier than it’s ever been. A few of the edges have been sanded down, the rooms all look far more distinct and memorable so it’s less of a mental maze, and the iron boots can be equipped and de-equipped simply by pressing down on the gamepad. Compared to the original, it’s a smooth ride. But still, this is one of the most involved dungeons in the game, so buckle on that Zora Tunic and let’s get swimming.

To enter the Water Temple, you need to be wearing (no, I wasn’t joking) the Zora Tunic, which you get by freeing King Zora from his ice block back in Zora Domain. To do that, you need Blue Fire in a bottle, which you obtain in the Ice Cavern as an adult. You’ll also need the Iron Boots from that same cavern. If you haven’t gotten that far yet, back yourself up and come back to this guide later.

Once you’re in the temple, remove your iron boots and float to the top. Run forward, and you’ll be in a big, open room with a bunch of doors on all four sides across four different floors, and water below you. I’m going to be referring to this as the “main hall” going forward. We’re going to be here a lot. I’ll also be referring to doors by floor and cardinal direction, so if you’re ever confused about what I’m talking about, open that map.

The first thing you’re going to do is slay that Blue Tektite in front of you. Sword, hookshot, whatever you like, just get it out of the way.

Next, you’re going to equip those Iron Boots, descend to the first floor, and head through the east door. Once you’re inside, remove the boots to float upward for a cutscene with your fiancee, Ruto. When it’s over, grab the Dungeon Map from the chest in front of you, and then stand on the platform with the Triforce symbol and play Zelda’s Lullaby to lower the water level.

Real quick tip: There are three different spots to mess with the water level in this dungeon, and each one will raise it to a different spot. This one brings it down to the first floor, and is marked by glowy blue pillars. It might be good to make a note somewhere of which color results in which water level, just in case you ever get turned around or confused and the water level is in the wrong place.

Once the water has dropped, fearlessly jump down the hole, landing in the water below so it doesn’t damage you. Now, with the water taken care of, note that one of the torches in here is lit. Stand behind it with your bow equipped and aim an arrow through the fire to each of the two torches on the other end of the room to open the door in front of you, then enter. Defeat the three Spikes by blocking their attacks with your shield and swinging your sword when they’re vulnerable to obtain a Small Key. (Din’s Fire is also usable for this puzzle).

Backtrack the way you came in and head out into the main hall. There are two more aptly named Spikes out here waiting for you; get rid of them. To your left (west side, 1F) is a bombable/breakable wall. Knock that out, and use your Iron Boots to sink, sink, sink. Head down the underwater corridor and take care of the Shell Blade by hitting it with your hookshot when its mouth is all the way open. At the end of the corridor, take off the boots and rise up.

In here, climb the platform jutting out of the water and use the hookshot to grapple onto the pillars ahead of you. Take down the Tektites and the Like Like (Hookshot is great here), and you’ll be rewarded with the Compass. Now, all the way back to the main hall!

To the west (1F) is a block you can push forward until it drops in the water. Do that, then use the boots to sink again. Look out for the Shell Blade in the way, then rise back up at the end of the hall. You’ll find yourself in a room with a whirlpool and some statues underwater, and a chest on the other side.

This looks trickier than it is. Use the iron boots to drop into the whirlpool, aiming for the center platform (the serpent’s body) stretched down the middle of the pool. Stand on the platform and use your hookshot to smack the weird gem thing in the serpent’s mouth. This will unlock the underwater grate, allowing you to enter. You might need to don and doff the iron boots a few times to swim over properly, but with some careful aiming it’s very doable. There are two more Shell Blades to fight inside, after which you’re free to take off the boots, rise to the surface, and grab the small key.

Back across the whirlpool, out the door, into the main hall again. There’s a locked door on the central pillar here, directly in front of you, and you now have a key. Head inside.

Once inside, step forward, turn around, and look up for a grapple point. Use your hookshot to get on the platform above. Right in front of you is a spot to stand and play Zelda’s Lullaby to raise the water back up. (Note: the columns here are green, green brings the water to 2F! Write it down if you need to!) Before you leave this inside pillar, though, jump in the water and look down: a new gap has opened up with the water level rising.

Use iron boots to drop down, where three Spikes are waiting for you. Kill them with the hookshot, then use the hookshot to hit the green switch above your head, releasing a Shell Blade. Kill that too, then remove the iron boots where it was standing to rise up into the now-open area. There’s a red rupee in a chest on the way up, as well as a gold skulltula on the wall opposite. Funny, I feel like something else used to be here in the original game…anyway, grab these, backtrack the way you came in, and take off the boots to rise back up into the middle of the pillar. Then swim out the door into the main hall.

Directly in front of you across the water (2F, east side) is a bombable/hammerable wall in an alcove. You know what to do. Smash the pots for bombs if needed, otherwise use the Megaton Hammer if you’ve cleared the Fire Temple already, and head on through.

Be cautious as you head into the water and equip the Iron Boots. There are four Stingers waiting for you. Your hookshot will take care of them, but it’s easy to get overwhelmed, so take it slow (not that you can do otherwise with those boots on). Once they’re dead, proceed to the end of the underwater hallway and unequip the boots to rise to the surface.

In front of you is a pool of water with a switch in it, and a round platform being lifted by a fountain. Use your hookshot to hit the switch to lower the platform, stand on the platform, then use hookshot again on the same switch to raise yourself up to the floor above. Your reward is a Small Key. Take it, then backtrack all the way to the main hall again.

In the main hall, hop in the water and swim around the central pillar to the locked door directly across from where you just entered. It’s got glowing red columns around itâcan’t miss it. Use the Small Key and head inside. Smack the switch and ready yourself; a blue Tektite will descend and try to fight you. Once it’s dispatched, stand on the lowered platform and use your hookshot to hit the switch again and rise up.

It’s another Zelda’s Lullaby spot, this time with red lights, that will bring the water to 3F! Play that same song! Up goes the water. Jump down from the ledge next to the Triforce symbol and back into the water of the main room.

Mind any Tektites you haven’t dispatched already and swim on over to 3F, west side, for another locked door for which you conveniently have a key.

A couple of Keese will be waiting for you to slash them up. Take stock of your surroundings before you proceed. You’ll want to use the hookshot to grapple your way up these moving platforms to the top, one by one. Start by dropping down to the platform below the one you start on, and it’ll descend until you can grapple across. Then just work your way up, one by one. Don’t panic, it’s very doable, and there’s a stable platform to the side you can jump to if you need a second to collect yourself. Head through the door at the top once you reach it.

In this next room, I started by hitting all the Tektites below with my arrows just to get them out of the way. Then, use your hookshot to hit the switch at the center of the room and make the grapple points raise up. You’ll want to grapple to the first point directly in front of you, then (counterintuitively) hit the switch again to lower the points. Use the grapple point on the next wall to crossâyou’ll be able to continue along the ledge only if the grapple point pillars are lowered. Hit the switch yet again and grapple to the final pillar, hit the switchÂ again to lower the pillars. Climb atop the one next to you, then hit the switch one final time to raise the pillar and lift you within reach of the next floor.

Hit those pots for health, arrows, whatever before you go through the door, it’s time for one of the best bosses in the game!

Shadow Link is tough; he mirrors your moves! Don’t attack him head-on while his shield is up or he’ll do something I hear the kids call “aura farming.” I was able to beat him pretty easily by keeping my guard up and dodging his combos, waiting for an opening. In the first phase of the fight, I would dodge to the left side twice whenever he swung, then would come in for a combo toward his exposed back. In the second phase, this was trickier, but still very doable if I dodged backwards twice and then threw out the hookshot to stun him. Make sure you dodge backwards far enough when he gears up for a spin attack, and don’t let yourself get pushed too close to the arena walls, or he can pin you down for some big damage.

With Shadow Link down, head through the door opposite where you came in (use your map if you got turned around) to finally obtain the Longshot, which will let you hookshot targets even farther away than before! To escape this room, aim for the very far away targets above you, then drop down the waterfall into the cavern below. Let yourself float down the river, but mind the whirlpoolsâyou can use the iron boots to just walk between them underwater. About three-quarters of the way down the river, there’s a gold skulltula on a wall above you. Make sure you’re firmly iron-booted underwater, then use the longshot to smack it once to defeat it, and then again to collect the token.

Climb atop the stone outcropping on the end of the river and use the longshot again, once to hit the eye above the gates and open them, and again to grapple to the grapple point on the other side of the gates before they close on you.

We’re back in familiar territory, so head out to the main hall once again. We’ve still got some work to do, and we’re sorely in want of a boss key right about now. First, we need to get the water level down. Get those boots on and drop down to 1F east, the room where you first ran into Ruto. Swim up to the Triforce and play Zelda’s Lullaby to drop the water, then head back into the main hall. Then, you want to enter the central pillar again from the door on 1F. We’re here to raise the water level, but hold up. Grapple to the Triforce stand, then look at the ceiling for a grapple point you can now reach with the longshot. Fly on up there, then look across to the other side of the ceiling for another gold skulltula. Once you have that, drop back down and play Zelda’s Lullaby.

Everything’s in place! Exit the pillar and run around the central platform to stand on the floating block, looking across at the outer wall. You should see a gate with a grapple point on the other side, and an eye switch above. Once again, use the longshot to hit the eye, then immediately use it again on the grapple to get through. Drop into the pool and equip the iron boots, then walk into the hallway. There’s a movable block wedged into the wall hereâpull it out as far as you can, blocking off the hallway, then take off the boots and swim up and out of the pool. You should be able to see a fence ahead of you with a waterfall on the other side, and a grapple point on the ceiling. Grapple to it with the longshot, use the iron boots to sink into the water, then head down the hallway and push the block out of your way to clear a path to the chest with yet another small key.

Back to the main hall again, equipping the iron boots again and sinking down to 1F, north. Grapple across the gap and use your small key on the door. Inside, there are a lot of tektites in the way. Use your sword, shield, bow, whatever, to get them out of the way (seriously, they will knock you into whirlpools otherwise). Carefully cross the room, avoiding the boulders, using the iron boots as needed to stay out of whirlpools, and go through the door. In here, use the longshot to dispatch all the stingers, then smash the rock walls (with bombs or the megaton hammer) to reveal a block that can be pushed into the water to hit a switch. The switch will raise the water level for you to head out the door in the wall above.

Whew. Almost done. In here, step on the switch to raise platforms you can jump across, and a tektite with them. Get rid of it (and the other tektite on the other side of the room) as you see fit, then cross and go through the opening. On the other side, be careful. Boulders are rolling down this stream and will run you over if you step in at the wrong time. Wait for one to pass, then go under the waterfall and immediately turn to the left to jump into the alcove. There’s a shell blade waiting, so get that out of the way. Before you head through the door, turn back, wait for the boulder to pass, then jump back in the stream, through the waterfall, and look upâthere’s another gold skulltula near the ceiling. Grab it fast, then turn back and head through the door to, at long last, claim the boss key.

Great work, folks! Turn back, follow the boulders down the stream and into the room you were in earlier with the boulders and whirlpools, then back into the main hall. I’m sorry to say we’ve got to mess with the water levels one more time. You’ll need to go back into 2F west, head up the fountain, and play Zelda’s Lullaby one last time to raise the water and open a path up the big stairs to the north on 2F (technically 3F). Drop down, and head that direction. You’ll have to stand on the platform of the central pillar and longshot your way over. Load up on hearts from the pots before you go in, and get ready to fight.

Okay, not yet. Use your iron boots in this room to climb the waterfall and avoid the spiky things spinning back and forth.Â Now get ready to fight!

Morpha isn’t too bad, as bosses go. The boss itself is the weird red Metroid-looking ball thingie that’s hiding in the tentacles. Your main goal is to use the longshot to rip it out of its watery protection and whack it a bunch with your sword. This is pretty easy with Navi’s targeting; the main thing you need to worry about is getting hit by water tentacles. Morpha will manifest these out of the water and try and slam them on the ground near you. Early on, they’ll be pretty spread out, but later in the fight they’ll form a big wall that’s harder to avoid. If you get trapped you can also use the grapple points on the sides of the room to fly out of danger. I tried to stay on the edges as much as possible; you never really want to be in the water with Morpha. But you can use the central platforms to bait Morpha into an easier spot for you to longshot the ball out, or just to cross out of the way of attacks. Try to be aware of where Morpha the Ball is at all times, as this fight can get annoying if it’s always all the way across the room, just out of range. Use those grapple points!

That’s the Water Temple done, folks! Enjoy the cutscenes, and get the heck outta there to see how the Zora are doing.

Oh, psst. Once the cutscenes are over, you’ll be at Lake Hylia, just before sunrise. Check out the stone monument in front of you to make good use of that timing. You’re welcome!

Michael Jackson: Blame Game Going On In Accusers' Battle Over Alleged Childhood Abuse

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

The legal battle between the entities in control of Michael Jackson's estate and the two men who claim they were sexually abused by the King of Pop is heating up ... and there's a lot of finger-pointing.

A Los Angeles judge sided with accusers Wade Robson and James Safechuck's move to stop Michael Jackson’s team from adding new arguments to their defense.

The lawyers for MJ's side were trying to amend their answers to the men's complaints so they could argue others are responsible for the alleged abuse of Wade and James ... and not MJ's companies.

In the filing, the lawyers adamantly dispute that Michael ever abused a child ... and point out Wade and James, and their parents, were among MJ's strongest supporters until well after his death.

The companies argued that, even if a jury believes the allegations of abuse, they should be allowed to make the argument that others who were not sued should also potentially be on the hook for damages ... including third parties who they say encouraged and facilitated Wade and James' friendships with the late singer.

Wade and James argued the defendants waited too long to add the argument ... and asked for the request to amend the suit be denied.  The court ended up denying the companies' motion to amend the complaint at this stage of the case ... but left the door open for them to file a renewed motion for leave to amend if appropriate down the line.

As TMZ first reported, Wade and James are seeking over $400 million in damages from MJ's companies.

Wade claims MJ abused him when he hung out with him after meeting him when he was 7 ... and James says he met the singer while working on a Pepsi commercial in 1986 and claims he was abused in the years after the introduction.

6 R-Rated Superhero Shows Better Than The Boys

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The superhero landscape looked very different back in 2019 before The Boys came around, as despite there being a fair few R-rated projects on the market, none were as crude or graphic as Prime Video's mega hit. Alongside putting a completely different spin on the genre by having mainly evil superheroes, The Boys also parodied Marvel, DC, and various other franchises.

However, with The Boys ending earlier in 2026, it left a big gap in the world of R-rated superhero shows, and while spinoffs such as Vought Rising and The Boys: Mexico will continue this universe, things simply won't be the same without the main series. Fortunately, there is already an abundance of great competitors out there.

Originally, it looked like Gen V would be The Boys' replacement before being canceled, but if you are looking for R-rated hits that are even better than anything this violent franchise produced, these six projects are definitely worth checking out.

Despite lasting just two seasons, The Punisher feels like a complete series that extends Frank Castle's story after first appearing in Daredevil. Getting to learn about the tragic events that turned him from a loving husband and father to a merciless killing machine is fascinating, and The Punisher certainly doesn't pull any punches with its violence.

As we've seen in the 2004 movie, characterizing Frank isn't always easy, as without the right nuance, he can feel empty and hard to connect with. However, Jon Bernthal delivers an outstanding performance that shows the humanity behind this vigilante, making it easier to root for him, despite not always agreeing with his actions.

The first season comes across as more personal, with Frank getting vengeance on those who were responsible for the death of his family, and the supporting cast is a key part of his journey. Ebon Moss-Bachrach, Amber Rose Revah, and Ben Barnes all do a great job with their respective characters, and while it can be an uncomfortable watch at times, The Punisher's story is ultimately very satisfying.

Season 2 presents a different vibe, with Giorgia Whigham's Amy Bendix bringing out the more fatherly side of Frank. Not only does he risk his own life to protect Amy, but he stands up for injustice outside his own personal grievances, with the final scene showing him fully embracing his Punisher persona.

Considering the character has been brought back as part of the MCU and even had his own special, it's clear Bernthal made a big impact as the Punisher, and the show successfully delivers exactly the type of brutal action needed to make it an all-time great that eclipses The Boys.

With James Gunn and Peter Safran stepping down from their leadership positions at DC Studios, the future of Lanterns is uncertain, but after just one season, it has already made a huge impression. Culturally, the show was massive, as it rejuvenated interest in the Green Lantern mythos and had people constantly bringing new theories and memes every week.

Sure, it definitely proved divisive as comic readers had major issues with the character and story direction, but as a whole, Lanterns was incredibly popular. The season 1 finale pulled in over 10 million viewers in just three days, and even those who hate the show can't deny how exceptional Kyle Chandler is as Hal Jordan, with Aaron Pierre shining opposite him.

Even though the series leans more into the mystery and detective genre than classic superhero elements, there are still several fun action sequences that definitely qualify it as a superhero project over something like The Penguin. Additionally, Lanterns sets up John Stewart to be more powerful in Man of Tomorrow, which should make up for the lack of major fight scenes.

It's rare that a modern show makes such a seismic impact in just nine episodes, but in terms of quality and consistency, Lanterns trumps The Boys, despite the latter having higher highs. The desire for a second season and Hal's return highlights how good the HBO series really is, and hopefully there's still a future for this surprise hit.

Much like Lanterns, Peacemaker's future is uncertain, even more so since James Gunn directed it, but it seems like season 2 was always planned to be the last anyway. Still, in just 16 episodes, Peacemaker manages to turn a bunch of obscure and original characters into fan favorites who audiences became 100% invested in.

John Cena's Peacemaker first appeared in 2021's The Suicide Squad, and while he was originally portrayed as arrogant and overconfident, viewers came around to him until he betrayed Rick Flag. That made centering a show around him extremely risky, yet Cena delivers a career-high performance full of emotion and vulnerability, adding so much depth to this anti-hero.

With an absolutely phenomenal cast for support, the series really thrives in its character work, making it almost essential that they return in future DCU projects. Both seasons feel incredibly different as they take place under completely different hierarchies, yet Gunn's vision allowed them to connect and achieve a similar quality.

Once again, it's a show that had people talking every week, predicting what would happen next, and somehow, people managed to guess season 2's most outlandish twist. At its core, Peacemaker is fun, hilarious, brutal, and surprisingly deep, cementing it as one of the greatest live-action superhero shows of the 21st century.

Its killer soundtrack only gives it more personality, and it has two of the best superhero show opening credits ever made, showing how much care was put into crafting this legendary series.

After the highly underrated 2009 movie, it wasn't clear how much legs the Watchmen franchise had in the highly competitive superhero market, but the TV show that premiered a decade later didn't disappoint. The series follows mostly different characters than the film, creating a unique spectacle, but one that is every bit entertaining.

Even though it's an original story, the tone and visuals capture what made the comics so great, and it isn't afraid to dive into culturally relevant topics. It tackles issues like racism in a mature manner, while having a compelling direction that isn't easy to predict, keeping viewers on their toes throughout all nine episodes.

Like the other entries on this list, the acting is top drawer, with Jeremy Irons standing out as the unhinged Ozymandias. The score is also worth mentioning, since it helps create the moody tone that makes Watchmen so gritty. That said, the show isn't afraid to get a little goofy at times either, with the humor separating it from DC's usual brand of comedy.

The story ties together nicely, and while the ending is packed, Watchmen still sticks the landing, making it no surprise it's so widely celebrated. It won 11 Emmys and was nominated for 26 in total, emphasizing the quality of this limited series, which didn't overstay its welcome.

Over 10 years ago, Daredevil debuted on Netflix, and to this day, many still see it as the greatest superhero show ever made. As a blind lawyer by day and a ruthless vigilante at night, Matt Murdock is the perfect protagonist for a dark, bloody show, and the three-season masterpiece couldn't have portrayed him any better.

Matt possesses all the charm and charisma needed to make him instantly likable, yet his disability and tragic origins cement him as a true underdog. Luckily, Foggy Nelson and Karen Page act as the optimal allies for him, which makes it all the more heartbreaking when he pushes them away, even if it's supposed to be for selfless reasons.

The drama is ever-present, and the choreography is perhaps still the gold standard for superhero projects, with that iconic one-shot hallway brawl remaining relevant all these years later. That's not to overlook Frank Castle's prison fight or the office brawl between Daredevil and Bullseye in season 3.

Speaking of Bullseye, he and Kingpin were so good that they both returned for Daredevil: Born Again with the same actors, confirming these villains were irreplaceable. Daredevil also has fantastic cinematography, with vibrant colors and shots helping forge an identity that the Disney+ show couldn't replicate, despite trying its best.

Between the fantastic cast, gripping storylines, flawless editing, and all the other qualities Daredevil possesses, it's no surprise it has such a stellar reputation. It is a superhero show that changed the genre forever, and while The Boys did the same, it simply doesn't measure up to Netflix's Daredevil.

While The Boys deserves a lot of credit for breaking the traditional Marvel and DC dominance by becoming one of the biggest superhero shows of the past decade, its Prime Video competitor did the same and is even better. Invincible arrived two years after The Boys, but the animated hit is far more consistent, and still has a healthy future ahead of it.

As a faithful comic adaptation, Invincible retells the story of Robert Kirkman's original series, albeit with some tweaks and additions. Its vast roster of characters is among its biggest strengths, as alongside having a fleshed-out, complex protagonist, there are so many wonderful heroes, villains, and other supporting allies that make the universe feel alive.

There is no holding back when it comes to fights either, as the gory injuries make even The Boys look tame at times. Invincible also does a great job of adding genuinely hilarious gags and jokes into its otherwise serious narrative, which is far less shocking or in your face than its Prime Video counterpart.

Some of its very best episodes have near-perfect ratings on IMDb, putting them among the greatest in TV history, and mixed with a fantastic soundtrack, A-list cast, and constant drama, there is no denying that Invincible is a better superhero series than The Boys.

The fact that the show managed to overcome its biggest weakness by shortening the gap between seasons to release regularly means it is bound to keep getting better, and with several seasons still to come for Invincible, it deserves to be recognized as one of the best in the genre.

Critically Acclaimed 2-Season Historic Drama Returns With Brand-New Release Mystery Fans Can’t Miss

Historical and history-inspired storytelling has been enjoying a thrilling multimedia boom thanks to international output, impressive budgets, and award-winning performances. Although not set in a real-world context, The Apothecary Diaries' blend of medical mystery and palace intrigue in an Imperial China-inspired backdrop has captured the hearts and minds of fans worldwide. From top to bottom with its elegant production quality and lovable cast, the series is heading through season 3 in full force, but this latest release is perfect for fans and collectors alike, with season 2 now out on Blu-ray.

Licensed by Crunchyroll, The Apothecary Diaries' latest Blu-ray box set for the complete season 2 is available via the Crunchyroll Store, Amazon, and other participating online and physical retailers as of November 10, 2026. The anime's critically acclaimed second season expands the political intrigue considerably, diving further into a plot against the central empire, teasing greater developments in the central romantic tension between dual protagonists Maomao and Jinshi. But as Maomao's new chapter has begun after her return to the pleasure district in season 3, while Jinshi expands his role while embracing the truth about his identity, it's the perfect time to recount everything from the past season with this latest release.

Featuring all 24 episodes of season 2, The Apothecary Diaries season 2 Blu-ray is available now on all major retailers just shy of two years after their debut in January 2025. The season expanded the plot immensely, escalating new conflicts with a rising faction vying for control over the country, with more than a few plot twists seemingly separating Maomao and Jinshi by the end. Fans can pick this box set up for $60 USD, with the following product details for those curious to learn more:

The Apothecary Diaries season 2's Blu-ray box set includes the following:

In addition to the regular Blu-ray collection, in two weeks Crunchyroll's licensed Blu-rays will also launch a SteelBook edition, and only $4 more at $64 USD, coming November 24. It's got all the features of the regular edition, but with two added collectible art cards as well as the gorgeous collectible metal case entailed. Each edition also includes key features like textless openings and endings, as well as all 24 episodes of the mini anime, Maomao's Monologue. It's a stellar reminder that, while physical media may indeed be reduced to a niche, it's not in danger of fully going away, especially with the genuinely excellent collector incentives that come with some of these new releases. The Apothecary Diaries is leading the charge at the moment, but everything from seinen romance, to iconic isekai, to genre-defining battle shonen anime are entering the field once more in the coming days, ready to throw a haymaker at everyone's wallets.

Stock Market Today, Nov. 10: Wall Street's Rally Hits a Roadblock as Hot Inflation Raises the Stakes for the Fed

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Yesterday's rebound lost momentum as rising Treasury yields and renewed inflation concerns weighed on stocks

Wall Street's rebound stalled on Tuesday, November 10, as hotter-than-expected inflation data reignited concerns about Federal Reserve interest rate policy and sent Treasury yields higher. The Nasdaq Composite (^IXIC -0.74%) dropped 0.74% to 25,066, retreating from Monday's gains, while the S&P 500 (^GSPC -0.38%) slipped 0.38% to 7,521 and the Dow Jones Industrial Average (^DJI -0.30%) fell 0.30% to 52,232.

Selling pressure extended beyond equities as gold fell 1.84% to $4,038.70, while the benchmark 10-year Treasury yield climbed to 4.88%, approaching the psychologically important 5% threshold. Market performance was mixed across sectors, with consumer cyclical stocks leading gains and industrials advancing, while healthcare lagged. The divergence highlighted an increasingly selective market as investors reassessed the outlook for interest rates and economic growth.

Despite the broader market decline, several prominent stocks posted strong gains on Tuesday. Meta Platforms (META +0.00%) surged more than 5% after JPMorgan (JPM +0.00%) added the social media giant to its Focus List, while Sea Limited (SE -1.49%) jumped 8.1% following stronger-than-expected quarterly revenue.

On the downside, Nvidia (NVDA -5.47%) plunged 5.52% amid an ongoing investigation involving CEO Jensen Huang, adding to uncertainty ahead of the chipmaker's upcoming earnings report.

Tuesday's sell-off underscores a growing challenge for equity investors heading into year-end. With inflation proving more persistent than expected, markets are increasingly pricing in another Federal Reserve rate hike in December. Higher borrowing costs could weigh on corporate profitability and put additional pressure on stock valuations, particularly among growth companies whose valuations depend heavily on future earnings.

However, the divergence in individual stock performance suggests investors are becoming more selective rather than abandoning equities altogether. Strong corporate earnings and favorable company-specific developments continue to attract capital, even as broader market conditions deteriorate. This could favor businesses with healthy balance sheets, strong cash flow, and the ability to sustain growth without relying heavily on external financing.

Looking ahead, investors should watch upcoming inflation data, Federal Reserve commentary, and Treasury yields for signs of whether monetary conditions will tighten further. With the 10-year yield approaching 5%, the sustainability of Wall Street's recent rebound may depend increasingly on corporate earnings delivering enough growth to justify elevated valuations.

DC Studios Exit: James Gunn and Peter Safran Spent Big, Lost Money and Didn’t Hook Audiences

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

After “Superman” landed in theaters last summer, former Warner Bros. Discovery CEO David Zaslav was ready to pop Champagne. Although the movie had only been playing on the big screen for three days, he touted the “bold 10-year plan” of DC Studios co-chiefs James Gunn and Peter Safran and promised “the DC vision is clear” and “the momentum is real.”

Fast forward a year and a half, and those plans have imploded. Gunn and Safran were dismissed from the top of the comic book empire, with Marvel Studios alum Jeremy Latcham taking over as president and chief creative officer of DC Studios. Related Stories Korea Box Office: Anime Hit 'Chiikawa' Retains Lead as Brad Pitt Thriller 'Heart of the Beast' Debuts Korea Box Office: Anime Phenomenon 'Chiikawa' Staves Off Corporate Thriller 'Final Interview' to Retain Top Spot

The duo’s ouster wasn’t a total shock, even though they had been assured they were part of David Ellison’s business leadership team at Skydance after Paramount’s $111 billion acquisition of WBD closed in October. Had the two stayed, the org change would have represented a demotion. Under the previous regime, they answered directly to Zaslav and not movie co-chairs Michael De Luca and Pamela Abdy. However, Ellison and co-CEO Ynon Kreiz made plans to shift Gunn and Safran’s reporting structure into new Skydance Motion Picture Group chiefs Dana Goldberg and Josh Greenstein.

The executive shuffle is yet another setback for DC Studios, which has struggled for years to compete with Disney’s Marvel Cinematic Universe. Their short-lived leadership stint at DC follows multiple turnovers over the past decade, starting with comic book veterans Geoff Johns and Jon Berg and then Walter Hamada. After DC’s last iteration of superhero movies flamed out with duds like “The Flash” and “Aquaman and the Lost Kingdom,” Gunn and Safran were hired in 2022 to overhaul some of the company’s most lucrative IP. Efforts to right the ship were mixed at best — with tentpoles and television shows that didn’t always generate enough excitement to justify their price tags. Sources suggest the thinking was they spent big — and lost too much — on projects that just weren’t resonating with audiences.

Gunn’s “Superman,” starring David Corenswet — the duo’s first big swing as bosses — was touted as a hit with $618 million against a $225 million budget. Those ticket sales were promising enough, given the general decline in popularity of comic book movies. But they didn’t necessarily signal the kind of lasting enthusiasm that spawns an interconnected universe. Momentum all but evaporated with July’s money-loser “Supergirl,” which flopped with $126 million globally against a $170 million budget. And while October’s “Clayface” performed modestly with $109 million against a $44 million budget, sources suggest the body-horror thriller won’t break even until the studio factors in downstream revenues.

Insiders have raised eyebrows at Gunn and Safran’s decision to focus on lesser-known characters, like Superman’s cousin Kara El-Zor, aka Supergirl, and the Batman villain Clayface, rather than building out the franchise with marquee names. Although superhero movies have been hit or miss, the ones that spotlight notable characters can still pack a box-office punch. July’s “Spider-Man: Brand New Day” became the third-biggest movie in history with $2.5 billion, and December’s “Avengers: Endgame,” which brings back just about all of Earth’s Mightiest Heroes, could challenge the web-slinging Marvel sequel’s box office records.

“The industry has taken away the wrong lessons from Marvel’s success,” a studio insider tells Variety. “Higher budgets don’t equal quality storytelling.”

Television efforts, too, were uneven. DC’s main small-screen success was “The Penguin,” a “Batman” spinoff led by Colin Farrell, was greenlit before Gunn and Safran took over. But the rest of the slate has generated less fanfare. Gunn’s “Peacemaker” series, anchored by John Cena, debuted in 2022 before he stepped into the studio job. Although initial season was popular, the second season – which launched three years later – failed to match the buzz of the first. “Lanterns,” their first live-action TV show, had posted decent viewership. Yet an insider at the company suggests the crime drama isn’t quite capturing the zeitgeist, pointing out the show didn’t get renewed for a second season.

“People cannot wait to make another season of a hit show,” the source said.

Safran, a steward of New Line’s wildly profitable “Conjuring” universe and producer on “Shazam!” and “Aquaman,” was used to cutting checks and poring over balance sheets before he was hired at DC. However Gunn, best known for directing Marvel’s “Guardians of the Galaxy” trilogy, was always a curious choice for the job. Gunn had a tricky balancing act, needing to shepherd his own directorial efforts while co-managing a slate. Collaborators praised Gunn’s ability to work quickly and efficiently on simultaneous projects. But even he admitted the challenge of needing to multitask and handle the “corporate” nature of the gig.

“The thing my ADHD hyperfocus has had the most difficulty with has been dividing my time between projects I’m incredibly passionate about and the ones I’m not so much and all the things I have to do as a studio head,” Gunn, who regularly converses with comic book enthusiasts on social media, wrote on X after his ouster.

The pair will remain involved with DC, though with less authority. Gunn is directing next summer’s “Superman: Man of Tomorrow,” and the two are producing the 2027 television series “The People v. Gorilla Grodd,” with Skyler Gisondo as his “Superman” character Jimmy Olsen and Jimmy Tatro as Gorilla Grodd, and another season of “Creature Commandos.”

It’s unclear what will become of Gunn and Safran’s decade-long creative strategy of sequels, spinoffs and stand-alone stories. After “Superman: Man of Tomorrow,” which returns Corenswet’s Man of Steel and Nicholas Hoult’s supervillain Lex Luther to the forefront, the only other movie in the pipeline is “The Batman Part II.” After several delays, the sequel is scheduled to be released in 2028 — six years after Robert Pattinson’s original Caped Crusader adventure. Their oft-teased “Wonder Woman” reboot never got off the ground. And though Gunn has hinted he’s writing another DC-related project, it’ll be up to Latcham to decide whether to continue with the vision of the prior regime.

Internally, there’s hope that Latcham — an executive who helped shape the MCU — will get back to basics with DC.

“Jeremy is the kind of guy who would go to Comic-Con to get an autograph from someone,” says another source, “not someone who wants to be there signing autographs.”

Galderisi vota Amorim: "Bisogna aspettare gli allenatori, oggi non si guarda più il lavoro giornaliero"

Ai microfoni di Tuttomercatoweb, Giuseppe Galderisi, ex giocatore tra le tante di Hellas Verona, Milan, Juventus e Lazio, ha rilasciato una lunga intervista in occasione del Premio La Clessidra ad Anghiari. Di seguito vari estratti delle sue parole anche sul Milan allenato da mister Ruben Amorim:

"Mi piace molto come allenatore e persona perchè diretto e schietto. Nel calcio bisogna avere pazienza ed essere bravi ad aspettare le persone in cui si crede. Amorim dipende da un pareggio, da un palo interno o da un palo esterno, non si guarda più il lavoro giornaliero".

Il Milan di Ruben Amorim corre più forte rispetto a quello guidato da Massimiliano Allegri nella scorsa stagione. Dopo 11 giornate i rossoneri hanno raccolto 24 punti, contro i 22 conquistati da Max un anno fa. Il successo sul Genoa di domenica ha portato il Diavolo al quarto posto, scavalcando il Como sconfitto a Milano contro l'Inter, tenendo a debita distanza Juventus, Napoli ed Atalanta ed accorciando sulla Lazio capolista. Amorim viaggia a 2,18 punti di media a partita, con una proiezione finale di 83 punti. Numeri incoraggianti, anche se il Milan deve trovare maggiore continuità e solidità a fine gara.

Max Dowman’s red card was upheld. Holder during the elimination of Arsenal by Fleetwood Town (D4) in the 1/8 finals of the League Cup (1-1, 5-3 tab), the 16-year-old winger was sent off in the 85th minute of play after an altercation with an opposing player. The England U19 international (10 caps) received a three-match suspension. “ The written reasons of the regulatory commission concerning its decisions will be published in due course », added the Gunners in their press release.

Grupo Cibest (CIB) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nequi's spinoff is complete and its loan book is expanding fast.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Grupo Cibest S.A. (CIB -3.96%) reported third quarter results that included loan growth acceleration and the completion of the Nequi spinoff. Management stated that commercial lending served as the primary driver of portfolio expansion as demand for new projects in Colombia increased. The company revised its full-year loan growth guidance upward to reflect this stronger demand. The transition of Nequi into an independent commercial financing company was completed on Sept. 1, and the entity now serves 29 million customers. Management indicated that capital allocation strategies, including the divestment of Banistmo and new capital instrument issuances, are intended to optimize the group's structure for long-term returns.

Operator: Good morning, ladies and gentlemen, and welcome to the Grupo Cibest Bancolombia Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Conference Call. My name is Carrie, and I will be your operator for today's call. [Operator Instructions] Please note that this conference is being recorded. Please note that this conference call will include forward-looking statements, including statements related to our future performance, capital position, credit-related expenses and credit losses. All forward-looking statements, whether made in this conference call and future filings in press releases or verbal address matters that involve risks and uncertainties.

Consequently, there are factors that could cause actual results to differ materially from those indicated in such statements, including exchanges in general economic and business conditions, changes in currency exchange rates and interest rates, introductions of competing products by other companies, lack of acceptance of new products or services by our targeted clients, changes in business strategy and various other factors that we describe in our reports filed with the SEC. With us today is Mr. Juan Carlos Mora, Chief Executive Officer; Mr. Mauricio Botero Wolff, Chief Strategy and Financial Officer; and Ms. Laura Clavijo, Chief Economist. I will now turn the call over to Mr. Juan Carlos Mora, Chief Executive Officer. Mr. Juan Carlos, you may begin.

Juan Uribe: Good morning, and thank you for joining Grupo Cibest's Third Quarter Conference Call. Please turn to Slide 2. In the third quarter, Colombia faced a difficult political, social and economic environment. A tense government transition was followed by a major earthquake in the Southwest. This increased fiscal concern and raise questions about how quickly and on what scale, reconstruction can restore infrastructure, economic activity and community well-being. Overall, we now expect the economy to have grown 2.1% in the third quarter, below earlier forecasts. Laura Clavijo will elaborate on this shortly. Even so, we delivered strong results.

Credit origination rebound sharply, led by commercial loans as confidence grew in the rule of law and security and as expectations rose for a credible path to fiscal adjustment. Net income reached COP 2 trillion in the quarter. Year-to-date, it is up 10.5%, more than offsetting Banistmo's contribution last year. NIM ended the quarter at 7.4%. The Investment income stabilized, time deposits resumed growth and lower spread commercial loans led the expansion on the loan book. Cost of risk rose 1.9% on higher consumer provisions. This was as expected and within our guided range. The strict expense discipline also underpinned solid operating performance. As a result, ROE for the quarter stood at 21.7%.

On September 1, we reached another corporate milestone. Separation of Nequi from Bancolombia. Nequi is Colombia's leading neo bank with a growing presence in Central America. It is now an independent commercial financing company, wholly owned by Grupo Cibest. This spinoff is designed to accelerate its development as a leading regional neo bank. In July, we also completed a intra-Group additional Tier 1 capital instrument transaction to further optimize Bancolombia's capital structure. I will now turn the call over to our Chief Economist, Laura Clavijo for an overview of the macroeconomic landscape. Laura?

Laura Clavijo: Thank you, Juan Carlos. If you could please proceed to Slide 4. Turning to Colombia, the economy enters 2027 with a mixed but gradually improving outlook. Our central view is that the year will be characterized by two competing forces, an improving investment cycle and post-earthquake reconstruction on the one hand and the necessary adjustment in fiscal and monetary conditions on the other. We expect GDP growth to moderate from 2.6% in 2026 to 2.4% in 2027. Household consumption should remain an important source of growth, but lose momentum as high interest rates, persistent inflation and softer labor market conditions constrained disposable income. At the same time, fiscal consolidation will reduce the contribution from government spending.

Encouragingly, the composition of growth should become more investment-oriented. This investment is projected to rise above 17% of GDP, supported by construction, mining and energy projects as well as reconstruction following the August earthquake. Inflation remains one of the key challenges, following a projected 6.7% year-end reading in 2026, headline inflation could peak at 7.3% during the first half of 2027, largely reflecting the impact of El Niño on food and regulated prices, together with indexation and the minimum wage adjustments. We expect these supply side pressures to fade during the second half, bringing inflation down to 5.5% by December. Nevertheless, still well above the Central Bank's target range. This inflation backdrop will keep monetary conditions tight.

We suspect the policy rate could rise further to 12.5% and remain at this level well into 2027 with the Central Bank waiting for clear evidence of disinflation before restarting the easing cycle. We, therefore, anticipate only modest cuts in the fourth quarter while fiscal risks and elevated global rates further constrained the scope for monetary easing. The fiscal position remains the main macroeconomic vulnerability. An ambitious economic rescue bill worth 2.4% of GDP, containing a significant budget cut and additional taxes are being discussed in Congress before year-end. The signals of fiscal responsibility but be weighed against significant expenditure rigidities and historically high financing requirements.

Our baseline scenario speaks of a Central Government deficit of 7.9% of GDP in 2027, compared with 7.2% in 2026 and assumes a fiscal adjustment of at least 1.5% of GDP to maintain investor confidence and set the path towards fiscal sustainability. Finally, external accounts remain manageable, although the current account deficit is expected to widen from 2.5% of GDP in 2026 to 3.2% in 2027. Importantly, remittances of around 3.4% of GDP should remain an important buffer, keeping the external deficit below its historical average of approximately 4%. Overall, 2027 should be viewed less of a year of acceleration and more of a year of transition.

Growth remains resilient, and its composition improves but Colombia will need to navigate sticky inflation, restrictive financial conditions and above all, a demanding fiscal adjustment before a more balanced and sustainable expansion can take hold. If you could please proceed to Slide 5. Turning to Central America. The outlook for El Salvador and Guatemala remains constructive, although inflationary pressures warrant attention. In El Salvador, growth accelerated to 5.1% year-over-year in the second quarter of 2026, increasingly supported by investment and double-digit construction growth. While resilient consumption, tourism revenues and renewed progress under the IMF program provide additional buffers despite lower remittances and higher import costs. In Guatemala, growth remained robust at 4.3%, supported by domestic demand, remittances, manufacturing and exports.

However, inflation has risen to 4.7%, while the medium-term outlook increasingly hinges on translating fiscal expansion and the strengthened public-private partnership framework into infrastructure investment. Alongside institutional improvements that could support the country's path toward investment grade. Now please let me turn the presentation to Mauricio, who will present Nequi as an independent entity followed by Grupo Cibest's quarterly performance.

Mauricio Botero Wolff: Thank you, Laura. Please proceed to Slide #7. Nequi was created 10 years ago with the purpose of improving the way people and business interact with money, and since its inception, its success as a powerful and scalable financial inclusion platform has been compelling with a customer base of more than 29 million users in an activity ratio above 80%. And Nequi was launched in 2016, offering a basic marketplace for top-ups and bill payments. Leveraging on the user's transactional data and cash flow dynamics. In 2019, Nequi launched its first credit product, a significant first step towards the financial evolution of individuals without prior credit history.

The pandemic marked a turning point in Nequi history, as it was one of the channels that the government selected to disburse subsidies, eventually driving fast, massive user engagement, expanding by 4.5x its user base in only 3 years. Since then, it has continued enhancing its marketplace and its value proposition has evolved from individuals towards small business cash management solutions and interoperability. Also, it initiated its regional expansion into El Salvador and Guatemala. Please turn to Slide 8. Its business model is built around three core services that foster engagement, generate high transaction volumes and capture a stable, low-cost operational deposits, complemented by a growing financial portfolio, a marketplace, B2B and cross-border solutions.

As of third quarter, 24 million customers are active users, out of which 18 million are monetized. Moreover, 7 out of 10 Colombian adults have a Nequi account and 3.9 million users of Nequi as their only account. 65% of Nequi account holders are not Bancolombia customers and 42% of payments in Bre-B ecosystem come from Nequi. In fact, exponential growth in user engagement and transaction volumes drove operating leverage and cost dilution, enabling Nequi to reach breakeven in late 2025. Please turn to Slide #9. From an accounting perspective, Nequi's stand-alone financials reflect its balance sheet as of September 30, while the income statement captures only 1 month of full operations as the spinoff was executed on September 1.

Thus, in the following slides, we provide a set of administrative financials and performance metrics for the third quarter and previous quarters on a stand-alone basis, intended only for comparative purposes. Deposits closed at COP 6.9 trillion, down 9.5% quarter-over-quarter as balances normalized of the midyear service bonus and up 24% year-over-year. On the other hand, its loan book expanded 12% in the quarter reaching COP 2.5 trillion. Following the spin-off, Grupo Cibest allocated COP 600 billion of capital to Nequi implying a total solvency ratio of 13%. All in all, Nequi initiates this new phase as an independent entity with a robust balance sheet to support scalable growth going forward. Please turn to Slide 10.

Net interest income increased 5.3% in the quarter due to loan expansion and a COP 20 million increase in interest income as a one-off related to the spin-off. Accordingly, NIM reached 18.1%. Absent of the one-off NIM would have been 16.8%. This represent a significant and diversified source of overall income and continued growing at a steady pace during the quarter. The net fee income ratio closed at 22.6%. From a credit loss perspective, provision expense amounted to COP 145 billion, an increase explained by higher loan origination in a one-off related to the transition between former credit model under local GAAP and the current IFRS accounting.

Thus, cost of risk reached 24.6%, which absent of the one-off, would have remained flat at 16.9% and in line with our expectations given the pace of loan growth. Please turn to Slide 11. ARPAC continues to grow while cost to serve continues to decline, such that ARPAC now represents 2.3x CTS demonstrating the strong operating leverage embedded in its business model. All in all, third quarter's net income was COP 33 billion, equivalent to a 23% ROE. Absent of one-offs related to the spin-off, net income would have reached COP 41 billion, which represents an ROE of 28%. Please turn to Slide 12.

Finally, we expect ROE to reach 28% in 2026 and between 28% and 30% in 2027, driven by loan growth that will support margin expansion while provision expenses evolve consistently with portfolio growth. Please turn to Slide 14. Now Grupo Cibest's gross loan portfolio reached COP 274 trillion, increasing 4.3% quarter-over-quarter and 9.7% and year-over-year, which absent of FX, would have been 13.5%, reflecting a strong underlying business dynamic. Commercial loans were the main growth driver, expanding 5.5% during the quarter as per a more constructive business environment in Colombia. Consumer loans also grew at a faster pace, increasing 2% in the quarter, mainly driven by unsecured personal loans and credit cards.

Meanwhile, mortgages maintaining their positive trend, expanding 2.1% over the quarter. Please proceed to Slide 15. When broken down by entity, Bancolombia continued to drive growth supported by commercial loans. The contraction in the consumer portfolio is explained by the spin-off of Nequi, while Nequi continued to show solid origination dynamics, growing 12% during the quarter. Bancoagricola maintained a positive trend, while BAM declined mainly in commercial loans, yet its consumer portfolio is showing signs of recovery with September posting the strongest month-over-month growth of the year. Cross-border lending continued to expand through our offshore operations, allowing us to provide competitive foreign currency financing across different geographies.

As of September, BAM's cross-border loan portfolio grew 63% year-over-year, while Bancoagricola expanded 13%. Please proceed to Slide 16. Total deposits reached COP 275 trillion, growing 1.5% quarter-over-quarter and 8.6% year-over-year. Excluding FX, annual growth would have been 13%. Bancolombia's growth came primarily from time deposits to offset part of the deposit migration resulting from Nequi's spinoff. The decline in savings accounts reflect seasonality related to payroll and employee benefit payments. At Bancoagricola and BAM, deposit growth was mainly driven by sight deposits. Please proceed to Slide 17.

Our funding mix remained resilient, supported by the strength of our sight deposit base, which represents 57% of total funding and provides a structural advantage in containing funding costs in the current interest rate environment. The cost of deposits increased slightly to 4.5%, reflecting Colombia's higher interest rate and the greater share of institutional savings accounts and time deposits. Please proceed to Slide 18. Net interest income reached COP 5.7 Trillion, decreasing 4.9% over the quarter, equivalent to a NIM of 7.4%. The drivers of the quarterly decrease are twofold. First, the normalization of treasury income following an exceptionally strong second quarter.

And second, lending NIM contraction due to higher funding needs related to Nequi's spin-off and to the positive dynamic of the commercial loan portfolio. Bancoagricola and BAM also reported NIM compression, mainly reflecting higher funding costs and lower lending and investment income, respectively. On an annual basis, however, while loans are growing at 9.7%, net interest income is increasing almost 16% and underscoring our robust intermediation capacity. Please proceed to Slide 19. Net fee income reached COP 1.4 trillion, increasing 1.6% over the quarter and 15.5% over the year and remains an important and diversified source of revenues. Fee income growth was broad-based.

However, bancassurance continued to stand out, growing 17% over the year and 3% over the quarter supported by the positive performance of our alliance with SURA. Moreover, fee expenses decreased both during the quarter and during the year, driven by ongoing efficiency initiatives. All in all, fee income ratio stood at 21%. Please proceed to Slide 20. Net provision charges reached COP 1.2 trillion, up 17% over the quarter, mainly reflecting an increase in a specific client segment in Bancolombia as well as the consumer segment, driven by higher originations. BAM recorded higher provisions following updates to its expected loss models, incorporating new information for the year while Bancoagricola's provision expenses remained elevated with higher returns adjusted by risk.

Overall, quarterly cost of risk stood at 1.8% and bringing the 9-month accumulated cost of risk to 1.7%. We continue to closely monitor recent events that could affect asset quality. While proactively managing exposures and addressing potentially affected clients. Starting with the earthquake in Colombia, asset quality in the affected areas remains in line with the overall local loan book, supported by relief measures and proactive client management. We may see some deterioration going forward as collections normalize, and we are building provisions accordingly with no expected impact on our cost of risk guidance range for either this year or next year. Regarding El Nino, our main exposure is concentrated in agribusiness, particularly agro exporters.

Provision expenses related to these exposures have already been recognized in line with expected credit losses. Given our limited exposure to potentially affected clients, we do not expect any material additional impact. Please proceed to Slide 21. Delinquency ratios remained well contained across portfolios during the quarter and reflect a meaningful reduction on a yearly basis. Moreover, from an expected losses perspective, Stage 1 has increased significantly its share, driven by growth on originations while Stage 2 and Stage 3 exposures declined over the year. Coverage remained solid at 131% and for 30-day past due loans in 195% for 90-day past due loans.

Overall, these trends indicate that the asset quality remains in line with our guidance considering the pace of growth. Please proceed to Slide 22. Operating expenses remained broadly stable, increasing only 0.3% quarter-over-quarter, driven by higher financial transaction taxes related to the ordinary and extraordinary dividend payments. On a year-to-date basis, expenses grew 8.7%, mainly reflecting the wealth tax recognized earlier this year. Excluding this effect, annual expense growth would have been approximately 5.2%, that is close to 1 percentage point below inflation. Overall, the consolidated cost-to-income ratio stood at 46.6%. We remain focused on structural efficiency and have launched different initiatives addressing our most significant operating and fee related expenses.

In parallel, our AI program is accelerating its adoption across the organization to enhance productivity, supporting a more scalable operating model. Please turn to Slide 23. Shareholders' equity reached COP 39 trillion, up 1.7% over the quarter. Mainly driven by earnings generation, while the year-over-year decline mainly reflects the Banistmo divestment. In July, Bancolombia issued COP 1 billion intra-group capital instrument contributing 44 basis points as additional Tier 1. Combined with the other intragroup transactions, this enhances the group's capital efficiency while providing additional flexibility to support growth in strategic priorities. Bancolombia's stand-alone solvency ratio remained relatively stable at 13.7% as earnings generation and the AT1 issuance helped offset the extraordinary dividend and growth in risk-weighted assets.

Finally, Cibest stand-alone double leverage increased to 98%, reflecting dividend distributions, yet preserving flexibility for corporate initiatives. Please turn to Slide 24. Net income reached COP 2 trillion, which represents an ROE of 22%. The accumulated net income increased 10.5% over the year despite the absence of Banistmo's earnings contribution. And consistently, ROE has jumped from 18% to 22% in the same period underscoring the rationale behind the divestment and the merits of the capital reallocation strategy. With this, I will now hand the presentation back to Juan Carlos. Juan Carlos?

Juan Uribe: Thank you, Mauricio. Please turn to Slide 26. Turning now to our guidance. For 2026, we are revising our loan growth outlook upwards. From a range of 7% to 8% to a range of 8% to 9%. This reflects stronger demand for commercial loans in Colombia. All other guidance items remain unchanged. For 2027, we project a slightly higher loan growth of 9% to 10%, again, led by commercial loans in Colombia, provided the fiscal deficit narrows in an orderly and gradually way. We also expect NIM of 7.5% to 7.7% and a cost of risk of 1.9% to 2.1%, reflecting stronger origination and interest rates that are likely to remain high.

Finally, we expect an efficiency ratio of 46% to 47%. We remain committed to a cost discipline while continuing to invest in our business transformation. Please turn to Slide 27. To conclude, if macroeconomic conditions to improve and investment confidence rebounds strongly, as we anticipate, Grupo Cibest is uniquely well placed to seize the growth opportunities ahead. And even in a more adverse scenario, our proven NIM management, some credit underwriting, cost discipline and efficient capital allocation allow us to deliver sustainable ROE throughout the economic cycle. With this, we conclude our prepared remarks. We will now be pleased to take your questions.

Operator: [Operator Instructions] And our first question will come from Ernesto Gabilondo with Bank of America.

Ernesto María Gabilondo Márquez: Thank you. Hi, good morning, Juan Carlos, Mauricio and Catalina, and thanks for the opportunity to ask questions. My first question will be on your earnings growth expectation for next year. So you have mentioned ROE could be maintained between 21% to 22% next year, but just wondering how should we expect in terms of earnings growth? And for my second question is on Nequi. If I am not mistaken, Nequi represents around 2% of Cibest consolidated net income. So how do you see the Nequi contribution to Cibest's earnings in the future? And wondering if you have any plans to IPO this business in the future? Or if we should just consider it as another subsidiary within Cibest.

Juan Uribe: Thank you, Ernesto. Let me take your question regarding Nequi, and I going to ask Mauricio to take your question about earnings. As you mentioned, Nequi today represents around 2% of Grupo Cibest net income. It's still small. Nequi will grow in the future, and we are very optimistic. But the size of Bancolombia is going to continue to be very important in Cibest. So Nequi will gain some importance in the future, probably in 2030 will be something around 8% to 10%. If the growth is strong, particularly on loans and depending on the credit cycle. But Nequi, it's an alternative.

I mean it's a diversification of Grupo Cibest, but Bancolombia will continue to be the most important entity for Grupo Cibest. But Nequi will add diversification, as I mentioned, and alternatives to have presence in the Colombian market. And also it's going to be an avenue of growth in other markets, particularly in Central America. Regarding the -- your question of our IPO, now Nequi is a separate entity fully owned by Grupo Cibest that give us the opportunity to assess different strategic alternatives. We are not thinking about an IPO in the short term.

But with this corporate structure, we have open alternatives, as I mentioned, to develop Nequi and to find the best way to give Nequi those avenues of growth. So we'll continue developing the business in Colombia and in the other markets that we are. And then in parallel, we will be assessing any additional strategic alternatives, but that is not going to happen in the short term is. Now regarding earnings, Mauricio?

Mauricio Botero Wolff: Ernesto, when we think about the end of this year, net income should be around COP 8.5 trillion. And that should be in the upper part of our ROE guidance. And when we think about 2027, the growth should be between 8% and 10% of that net income. So if you think about either COP 9.2 trillion, COP 9.3 trillion, that should give you the 21.5%, 22% ROE guidance that we're thinking for next year.

Operator: Our next question will come from Tito Labarta with Goldman Sachs.

Daer Labarta: Two questions also. I guess, first on the loan growth, as you mentioned, commercial lending seems to be accelerating, I guess, on the back of the elections, just to think about the sustainability of the loan growth, particularly given, as you mentioned, you need the fiscal adjustments to happen. I think Juan Carlos, you mentioned as long as that kind of happens gradually. So how do you think about that fiscal adjustment? How do you think -- what are the upside, downside risk to loan growth depending on how that evolves. Also, I guess, on the consumer lending, which has lagged a little bit. Do you think that can accelerate?

Is that maybe more second half of '27 as there's more visibility on the fiscal adjustment. So just to think about the sustainability of the loan growth, given some of the macro risk that are still there for Colombia. And then second question, just on the sustainability of the ROE. A $35 Million Home Just Hit San Francisco’s Market, Marking the Most Expensive Listing of the Year

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

San Francisco’s most expensive home of the year just hit the market and is expected to draw interest from potential buyers flush with cash from the AI boom.

The home at 2770 Broadway officially went on the market on Monday with a nearly $35 million price tag.

The seven-bedroom, 6.5-bathroom home is already drawing inquiries, listing agent Neil Ward of Compass told SFGate.

Located in the exclusive Gold Coast neighborhood, the home sits on a 50-foot-wide, double-width lot, with breathtaking views of the San Francisco Bay, Golden Gate Bridge, Palace of Fine Arts, and other famed landmarks, according to the listing.

The home was designed in 1894 by Reid & Reid, the same architectural firm responsible for the iconic Fairmont Hotel. While many Gold Coast homes of this age, most of which are generational properties, need complete renovations after being sold, Ward told SFGate that the potential buyer of 2770 Broadway can “walk in with their toothbrush and their furniture.”

A 2007 renovation by architect Jim Jennings and Van Acker Construction brought together the home’s original turn-of-the-century grandeur with the functionality needed for modern family living.

The home’s 9,000 square feet are spread across four floors, where prospective buyers will find herringbone-patterned floors, arched openings, and fine millwork, all evidence of the home’s architectural greatness.

The property also features a two-car garage, wine cellar, and sport court in the rear of the house.

The home was purchased by San Francisco business executive David Chamberlain in 1989, according to SFGate. He was CEO of companies such as Shaklee, Stride Rite, and Genesco, but also had a deep connection with his city, serving as president of the San Francisco Opera and the San Francisco Chamber of Commerce.

After his death earlier this year, Chamberlain’s two daughters decided to sell the home.

Ward told SFGate that he thinks the property will sell for its $35 million asking price, which would make it the most expensive home sold on the multiple listing service in San Francisco this year.

“It is a really, really rare product that does not hit the market,” Ward said.

Between the home’s turnkey condition and the city’s competitive ultraluxury home market, Ward thinks the home’s time on the market will be brief.

“I’ve done this for over 35 years. I was here for the dot-com boom and everything in between. Nothing comes close to what’s taking place right now,” Ward said.

San Francisco is experiencing what some real estate brokerages are calling a “mansion shortage,” according to NPR. The AI boom, much of which is concentrated in San Francisco, has caused demand for luxury single-family homes in the area to skyrocket, while the supply remains stagnant.

Home to major AI companies such as OpenAI and Anthropic, the San Francisco market had a median of just 37 days on the market, according to Realtor.com data, two weeks less than the national median of 61 days.

Some listings have had over 50 offers, and 44 homes were sold for at least $1 million over asking in June alone, according to a report from NPR.

In October, the sale of a home in San Francisco’s Presidio neighborhood broke the record for the highest sale above asking. The 1966 midcentury home was listed for $18.5 million and sold for $28.5 million, an eye-watering $10 million premium.

Ross Douthat’s ’60 Minutes’ Interview Gets Tackled By Football Comparisons

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Ross Douthat was dynamite. The ratings — potentially less so.

The new “60 Minutes” correspondent garnered many huzzahs for his interlocution of President Donald Trump on the show’s Sunday telecast, but viewership fell notably from the previous week and from the year-earlier episode, both of which were boosted by NFL lead-ins.

Even so, there is some cause for cheer. In a season when the show has faced challenges luring viewers without an NFL game before it, the ratings were the highest generated without a football telecast to help kick things off. Related Stories Quentin Tarantino Thought He Was ‘Letting Down’ Brad Pitt by Not Directing Cliff Booth Sequel: ‘I Felt Bad About That’ Korea Box Office: Anime Phenomenon 'Chiikawa' Staves Off Corporate Thriller 'Final Interview' to Retain Top Spot

Douthat peppered Trump not only with questions tied to the loss Republicans suffered in the recent midterm elections, but also stopped him with inquiries about whether the president felt he would go to heaven or hell, and whether he could refrain from taunting and insulting his opponents it it means doing something for the greater good of the nation.

The literati noticed, with notables like Fox News commentator Brit Hume and CNN media analyst Brian Stelter praising Douthat’s interview.

But the show’s audience for its eighth episode of the season fell to 7.8 million viewers overall, and 918,000 between the ages of 25 and 54 — the demographic coveted most by advertisers. The overall figure marked a 26% decline from the previous week, when an NFL football game airing before the show drove 11 million viewers to the program. And the demo audience tumbled 69% from the previous year, when the show won nearly 2 million viewers between 25 and 54.

The numbers are also down from the same episode from the previous week, when the telecast lured an average of nearly 10.5 million overall and 2.23 million in the demo — representing declines of 30% and 59%, respectively.

Both the week-earlier and season-earlier episodes were lifted by NFL games that aired ahead of the first tick of the “60 Minutes” stopwatch. Sunday’s program was not.

CBS News remains under intense pressure to demonstrate stability at “60,” which was overhauled in haphazard fashion at the end of last season, despite mustering a notable 9% uptick in overall viewership. Bari Weiss, CBS News’ editor in chief, ousted Tanya Simon, an executive producer with years of history working at the show, in favor of Nick Bilton, a journalist and screenwriter with less formal TV experience. Also pushed out were correspondents Scott Pelley, Cecilia Vega and Sharyn Alfonsi. Anderson Cooper left of his own volition following the end of his contract. Their replacements, which include Douthat and contributors like Ariel Levy and Sebastian Junger, have top-shelf backgrounds, but also little experience working on a weekly TV program that emphasizes long-form enterprise journalism.

Sunday’s telecast also included an appearance by Trevor Phillips, who was recently named senior global affairs correspondent for CBS News. He interviewed far-right National Rally leader Marine Le Pen amid protests by French citizens.
