Kia launches two new PV5 electric van variants in the UK

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Kia opened orders for two new PV5 electric van variants, the PV5 Chassis and PV5 Cargo L2/H2, in the UK.

The PV5 is Kia’s first fully electric van from its Platform Beyond Vehicle (PBV) business. Built on Hyundai Motor’s dedicated modular skateboard platform, E-GMP.S, Kia can easily swap body styles to enable “limitless possibilities.”

So far, the PV5 has mostly been available in Passenger and Cargo body styles with several different variations.

Kia added the PV5 Chassis, an open-bed model that can be converted into just about anything. In the UK, Kia is offering conversions through TGS Automotive Group, including lightweight tipper, Dropside, and Box solutions.

The PV5 Chassis is available exclusively with the long-range 71.2 kWh battery in the UK, which Kia said could offer a potential WLTP range of up to 258 miles (416 km).

For reference, the PV5 Cargo L2/H1, which uses the same 71.2 kWh battery, offers a WLTP range of up to 258 miles (416 km). Other markets will also have a standard-range 51.1 kWh option.

The Chassis model is 4,610 mm long, 1,895 mm wide, and 1,935 mm tall, with a 2,995 mm wheelbase.

Its compact size and 5.5-meter turning radius, Kia said, make it easy to maneuver in city streets, construction sites, and other tight work areas.

Inside, the PV5 Chassis features two seats. Like other variants, it includes a 12.9″ navigation screen and a 7.5″ driver display with standard wireless Apple CarPlay and Android Auto.

The Chassis model supports DC fast charging up to 120 kW, enabling the battery to charge from 10% to 80% in about 30 minutes. Using an 11 kW AC supply, it takes about 6 hours and 30 minutes to charge from 10% to 100%.

Payload depends on the configuration. Kia said the Dropside offers a payload of up to 633 kg, while the Tipper can carry 561 kg.

In the UK, the PV5 Chassis is now on sale from £32,916 ($43,400) on the road, including the expected £5,000 ($6,700) UK Zero Emission Van Grant.

However, it’s an “incomplete vehicle,” as Kia describes it. The converted PV5 Dropside is priced from £40,110 ($53,500) on the road, including the ZEVG, while the PV5 Tipper starts at £41,310 ($55,000).

Kia also launched the larger PV5 Cargo L2/H2 in the UK. The new variant is 4,695mm long, 1,895mm wide, and 2,200mm high, with a 2,995mm wheelbase.

The taller roof opens up an internal cargo-area height of 1,815mm. It also offers a greater load capacity than the L1/H1 and L2/H2, with 5.2 cubic meters.

It’s available in three- and four-door configurations with the choice of two or three seats. The three-door versions have a maximum payload of 630 kg, while the four-door models have dual sliding doors and offer a payload of up to 600 kg. Both versions can tow up to 1,500 kg.

Like the Chassis model, the PV5 Cargo L2/H2 is available in the UK exclusively with the 71.2 kWh battery, which provides a WLTP range of up to 239 miles.

Inside, it features the same setup as the other PV5 variants, including a 12.9″ touchscreen navigation screen and a 7.5″ driver display.

The base PV5 Cargo L2/H2 Essential two-seat starts at £35,016 ($46,700), on the road, including ZEVG.

Grading the trade: Josh Downs gives Chiefs WR help they needed, but is he the right fit?

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Just prior to the trade deadline, the Kansas City Chiefs acquired wide receiver Josh Downs from the Indianapolis Colts. The Chiefs will receive Downs and a 2027 seventh-round pick, and the Colts will get back fourth- and fifth-round picks in 2027.

Downs, 25, who recently requested a trade, got his wish and now will team up with Patrick Mahomes. In nine games (seven starts) with the Colts this season, Downs caught 33 passes for 354 yards and one TD on 57 targets.

Downs also had a team-high seven catches for 72 yards on nine targets in the Colts' 33-30 loss in Kansas City back in Week 2.

Who won this trade? Let's dive into the debate.

The Chiefs were in big need of adding receiver help. Not one wideout on the roster has either 50 targets or 300 yards receiving on the season. Downs, sharing the lead role in Indy along with Keenan Allen (and with Alec Pierce hurt), has surpassed both marks.

That's not to say that he instantly will become Patrick Mahomes' go-to target. But it's hard not to imagine Downs stepping in and earning a fairly sizable role in Kansas City's offense, assuming he can develop a relationship with Mahomes and pick up the playbook quickly.

Downs has primarily been a slot receiver in his career, although his work rate playing outside has increased slightly in 2026 out of need. It would make sense to put him in the slot, although what makes him, Rashee Rice and Xavier Worthy attractive is that they can line up in multiple spots.

Rice's numbers over the past year-plus have been slightly better inside, but Worthy has been far more efficient as a perimeter receiver in his career. That likely means Downs can take those snaps and do what he does best: run efficient routes, separate quickly and generate first downs. The biggest drawback is that Downs is not a deep threat, which was probably their biggest need.

This move also can help the Chiefs' punt-return situation. Nikko Remigio has struggled in that role this season. Downs has only returned two punts this season, but he has a very respectable 9.8-yard career average on punt returns.

The Chiefs know that Downs is a free agent after the season, and they had to be comfortable with the idea of this trade potentially being a half-season rental. Thinning out their Day 3 pick allotment stings a little, but the price wasn't that steep in the big picture, especially when you compare it to what the Seahawks paid for Rashid Shaheed (fourth- and fifth-round picks) and what the Jaguars dealt for Jakobi Meyers (fourth- and sixth-rounders) before last year's deadline.

This move is about taking advantage of a championship window with Mahomes showing he's recovered from his knee injury and Travis Kelce still performing at a relatively high level in his expected swan-song season.

Downs requested a trade and got his wish. The Colts likely weren't convinced they had to extend him, and Downs probably wasn't returning to Indianapolis next season anyway, so trading him now made perfect sense instead of waiting for compensatory-pick returns more than a year from now.

It fits right in line with the other trades they've made since firing GM Chris Ballard, shipping out one-time franchise pillars Quenton Nelson and DeForest Buckner and also dealing Allen. When you consider what they got back for Downs, which was two early Day 3 picks, it was a pretty fair market price.

Not having a first-rounder next spring from the Sauce Gardner will continue to sting. But with the trades, they've managed to replenish the cupboard a bit. They already have seven Day 3 picks to work with and are projected to receive two more compensatory selections in that same range.

Having those picks can allow the Colts potentially to slide up in the draft, and quarterback could be on the menu given the uncertainty surrounding Daniel Jones, even if Jones was not traded by the deadline.

They'll also eventually need to replenish a gutted WR room, but for now the Colts can see what they have in the likes of Nick Westbrook-Ikhine, Anthony Gould, Ashton Dulin, Deion Burks, Laquon Treadwell and, of course, Pierce, when he's back healthy again. It wouldn't be stunning if the Colts only bring back a few of these players next year, but the remainder of the season will help them decide who stays and who goes.

Liverpool, addio Van Dijk? In scadenza a giugno: "Se si prolunga, tanto meglio. Altrimenti..."

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Virgil van Dijk mantiene la massima serenità in merito al proprio futuro al Liverpool, nonostante il suo contratto con i Reds sia in scadenza il prossimo giugno. L'obiettivo prioritario è restare focalizzato esclusivamente sulle prestazioni in campo per la stagione in corso.

Van Dijk ha voluto rassicurare l'ambiente sulle sue condizioni e sul suo stato d'animo, spiegando di voler continuare a giocare ad alti livelli e di non avvertire alcuna pressione legata al rinnovo contrattuale.

Non c'è nulla da segnalare per il momento, ma questo non significa che sono in panico. Mentalmente sto bene; fisicamente ho avuto un piccolo problema in queste ultime due settimane, ma sto facendo tutto il possibile per essere presente sul campo". November Patch Tuesday: An Overflowing Cornucopia of CVEs

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Microsoft serves up an actively exploited Outlook zero-day, and a wave of critical Exchange, SharePoint, Windows Search, and LDAP bugs that exhausted defenders need to prioritize fast.

November offers up a veritable Patch Tuesday feast of vulnerabilities that are more likely to be exploited. In its second four-digit Patch Tuesday release in a row, Microsoft today addressed 1,093 security vulnerabilities, with 63 rated critical, and one zero-day in the mix (CVE-2026-124194, CVSS 6.5) that Microsoft confirmed is under active exploit.

"Most security teams haven’t quite recovered from the last month of bumper-to-bumper critical vulnerabilities, emergency patches, and '2shell'-branded everything, and we're yet again at another Patch Tuesday," says Jake Knott, head of threat intelligence at Watchtowr.

"Teams are really starting to feel the strain of working out what actually matters when there is fire after fire every day demanding immediate attention," he adds. "Our advice hasn't changed … start with what you actually have exposed to the Internet, then what's already being exploited in the wild, and only then everything else. Before going into panic mode, check whether you even use the vulnerable component, or meet the prerequisites for exploitation."

Related:OnlyFans Copyright Takedowns Expose Compromised Websites

To that end, security teams should prioritize CVE-2026-124194, and not just because attackers are already targeting it. It's a spoofing vulnerability in Outlook that may not allow code execution, hence its moderate severity rating, but could allow the disclosure of an account’s NTLMv2 hash — and in turn, the target's login.

"To exploit it, an attacker sends a target a malicious Office file and convinces them to open it," explained Satnam Narang, senior staff research engineer at Tenable, in his monthly Patch Tuesday commentary. "If the attack succeeds, the attacker captures the victim's NT LAN Manager v2 (NTLMv2) hash, a value derived from their Windows password. The attacker may be able to relay it to other systems to sign in as the user, or crack it to recover the password."

Security teams should also prioritize a pair of critical remote code execution (RCE) vulnerabilities, in Exchange and SharePoint, respectively, that each open targets to any attacker with a low-privilege login.

The first, in Exchange Server (CVE-2026-118207, CVSS 8.8), "belongs in this week's emergency window for every Exchange Server Subscription Edition server you run," Automox researchers warned in their monthly Patch Tuesday bulletin, and "all an attacker needs to start is a low-level login."

It's an improper input validation that could allow an authenticated attacker with low-level access to send the server a specially crafted request and run code with no user interaction.

Related:The Evolving Cybersecurity Lexicon: Vulnerability Discovery to Resilience

"Code on an Exchange server reaches every mailbox it hosts," warned Automox researchers. "And Exchange usually holds broad rights in Active Directory, so the mailboxes are just the start."

Meanwhile, an important-rated flaw (CVE-2026-120002, CVSS 9.9) in SharePoint Server is an elevation of privilege vulnerability that needs quick addressing, according to Automox.

"The flaw is incorrect authorization," the researchers noted. "That 9.9 comes from a changed scope: a successful attack reaches past the vulnerable SharePoint component itself. An attacker who already holds a low-privileged account sends requests over the network and elevates their privileges, with no user interaction required."

Another critical RCE to watch out for, according to Automox, is CVE-2026-110043 (CVSS 8.8/10), a one-click heap-based buffer overflow in Outlook that uses the Preview Pane as an attack vector. A target must select a weaponized email so it displays in the Preview Pane for it to trigger the attacker's code, which runs with that user's permissions.

"Patch internet-facing Exchange and SharePoint servers, and book the Exchange restart now," the Automox researchers advised. "Push the Office update this week, then check the installed build, because a fully patched Windows dashboard says nothing about Office. Rank by score and assessment, not label."

Related:Ivanti ICS Zero-Day Attacks Highlight Vendor Patching Hurdles

Dustin Childs, head of threat awareness at the Zero-Day Initiative (ZDI), also identified a Windows Search RCE (CVE-2026-116928, CVSS 9.8) as needing immediate attention.

"We start with Windows Search, which is reached through Exchange Server," he said in his November Patch Tuesday blog post, where he also listed 30 potentially wormable CVEs to patch quickly. "This bug allows an unauthenticated attacker to send an email with a specially crafted attachment that triggers the bug when the server automatically indexes it. The recipient never opens anything. It is a zero-click code execution bug on a high-value target. It’s also a CVSS 9.8 and clearly more severe than any of the nine patches Exchange receives this month."

And finally, Childs pointed to CVE-2026-119958, an RCE bug in Windows Lightweight Directory Access Protocol (LDAP).

"This patch addresses a bug that allows an unauthenticated attacker to exploit an out-of-bounds (OOB) write (CWE-787) by sending crafted LDAP calls over the network, with no user interaction," he warned. "Since it runs code in the LDAP service context, it could be quite destructive, especially on Domain Controllers. Test and deploy this one quickly."

Childs also recommended testing and deploying patches for the wormable bugs quickly, especially for network-facing services like DNS, DHCP, LDAP, and Exchange.

White the cornucopia of CVE this month, especially on the heels of the record October Patch Tuesday, may feel overwhelming, security teams should stick to best practices and not get overwhelmed, advises Tyler Reguly, associate director of security R&D at Fortra.

"I don't know about everyone else, but I'm tired," he said via email, adding that he believes that, eventually, AI will finish turning up old bugs and teams can once again look forward to smaller Patch Tuesday releases. "I can only imagine that if I’m tired, admins and operations teams everywhere are also tired. I believe that we will make it through to the other side, if we prioritize updates and focus on what really matters. The noise is coming at us from all sides, and we need to cut through it and figure out what really matters to the business."

That is according to Fabrizio Romano, who reports that the speculation around the former Chelsea and Tottenham head coach moving to Old Trafford has “gone cold”.

The Italian had been heavily linked with a switch to the north west in recent weeks as pressure piled on Michael Carrick after a dismal run of form saw the Red Devils win just once in 11 matches, as they lie in the relegation zone of the Premier League table.

Conte, who most recently coached Napoli, is thought to be keen on the United job, and his name was bandied around with other potential candidates, such as former Newcastle boss Eddie Howe and ex-Bayern Munich and Germany head coach Julian Naglesmann.

However, in the last couple of days, another ex-Chelsea and Spurs manager, Mauricio Pochettino, has overtaken all three of those names to become the favourite to succeed Carrick.

Romano reported on Tuesday that the reason behind United opting against Conte was because that the club were slightly “scared” of the 57-year-old.

"There is a feeling within the industry that Manchester United are 'scared' of Conte,” he explained on his YouTube channel.

‘He just goes and says the reality, he's a serial winner but brutal. I think this is exactly what United need. You need to understand the real problems instead of just hiding."

It is believed that United’s hierarchy are in Monaco to hold crisis talks over the manager’s future on Wednesday, with a decision expected on Carrick’s sacking on Thursday.

Ilhan Omar huddles with her DC-based lawyers during her first day back on Capitol Hill amid federal probes

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Rep. Ilhan Omar, D-Minn., told Fox News she is not concerned that the federal investigations into her immigration status and financial filings will distract her from serving her constituents in Congress following a lengthy meeting with her lawyers.

Rep. Ilhan Omar, D-Minn., returned to Capitol Hill this week and brought two lawyers with her after a scandal-plagued fall recess.

Fox News spotted the two attorneys exiting her Washington, D.C., office Tuesday after what appeared to be a roughly two-hour meeting with the congresswoman.

Omar hired two high-profile attorneys, Amy Jeffress and Joshua Matz, both of whom are partners at Heckler Fink LLP, amid dueling investigations. After emerging from Omar’s office on Tuesday, Fox News asked about their confidence in Omar’s defense against the federal government.

OBAMA ADMIN FIRST LAUNCHED THE INVESTIGATION INTO ILHAN OMAR'S ALLEGED BROTHER MARRIAGE

The attorneys were also asked about the recent news that both the Obama and Biden administrations had been the first to investigate Omar after she claimed that the Trump administration was politically targeting her.

Rep. Ilhan Omar, D-Minn., speaks during a news conference Wednesday in which she blamed President Donald Trump for death threats and attacks against her. (AP Photo/Adam Gray)

This is the first sighting of Omar back on the Hill after two federal departments opened investigations into the Somali-born congresswoman looking into both her immigration status and previous financial filings.

In October, agents with Homeland Security Investigations (HSI) raided her Washington, D.C., home as part of what Fox News Digital learned was an investigation into whether she or her father committed fraud when filling out her immigration paperwork.

She spent the final weeks of her campaign asking for money to defend herself in the investigations and accusing Trump of targeting her for political reasons.

About an hour after Omar's lawyers exited her office Tuesday, the congresswoman departed with husband Tim Mynett.

She was approached by Fox News about the ongoing investigation and asked whether she is concerned it could affect her ability to effectively represent her constituents in Congress.

She was then asked if there was any chance she would step down from Congress despite just winning re-election to let the investigation "play out."

The congresswoman smiled and waved at the camera but did not respond as the elevator doors closed with her inside.

SCANDAL-PLAGUED ILHAN OMAR EASILY SECURES 5TH TERM IN DEEP BLUE MINNESOTA DESPITE IMMIGRATION FRAUD PROBE

Rep. Ilhan Omar, D-Minn., pictured walking and talking in the U.S. Capitol basement. (Fox News Digital)

Omar also refused to respond to Fox’s question about whether she had spoken to House Minority Leader Hakeem Jeffries about the investigation.

The attorneys seen exiting her office earlier, Jeffress and Matz, have both represented high-ranking Democratic elected officials and have taken on multiple cases against President Donald Trump, his allies and supporters.

Jeffress served as former President Joe Biden’s attorney in his lawsuit against the Justice Department earlier this year. She also served as counsel for then-House Speaker Nancy Pelosi and the House committee investigating the Jan. 6, 2021, riot on Capitol Hill.

She is also married to U.S. District Judge Christopher Cooper of the District of Washington, who ruled in May that Trump's addition his name to the John F. Kennedy Performing Arts Center was illegal.

UFC CEO Dana White listens as President Donald Trump speaks in the James S. Brady Press Briefing Room at the White House in Washington, D.C., Nov. 9, 2026. (Reuters)

Matz served as counsel to House Democrats during both failed impeachment attempts against Trump during his first term in office. Matz also represented E. Jean Carroll in two civil cases against Trump’s denial of her sexual assault claims against him.

Matz also represented those opposed to the Florida Parental Rights in Education Act, which banned teachers from teaching students about gender identity and sexual orientation.

Omar has not yet formally announced a meeting with any members of Congress about the two ongoing federal probes. Her only formal address to the public on the investigations was posting to social media, including a video in which she encouraged her supporters to keep sending money in light of the new investigations against her.

‘Tangles’ Cast Members Seth Rogen, Julia Louis-Dreyfus & Bryan Cranston On Animated Film’s Rare Back Story

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

An animated film takes time to make. An animated film based on a funny-but-deeply-moving graphic memoir about a family’s experience with early-onset Alzheimer’s disease can take a bit longer.

Lauren Miller Rogen, wife of Seth Rogen and one of the producers of the film Tangles, told the crowd at Monday night’s special screening of the film in New York that work began on the adaptation back in 2016. It was acquired earlier this year by IFC, which will release it for a qualifying run later this month ahead of a wider bow in early 2027.

“I saw so much of my own story in [author] Sarah Levitt’s family story,” Rogen said. “I have a feeling a lot of you will see your own stories in it as well.” Related Stories News AMC Global Media Posts Mixed Q3, Previews 'The Walking Dead' AMC+ Bow News Martin Scorsese Explains How Seth Rogen "Ruined" His 'Cleopatra' Film: "Kim Jong Un Doesn't Have A Sense Of Humor"

The regrettable ubiquity of dementia and Alzheimer’s, along with the potency of Levitt’s work, drew a crowd of A-listers to the project. The voice cast includes Seth Rogen, Bryan Cranston, Julia Louis-Dreyfus, Abbi Jacobson and Beanie Feldstein. Producers include Louis-Dreyfus; Rogen via his company, Point Grey; Monarch Media, Giant Ant; and LYLAS Pictures. Watch on Deadline

During a Q&A after the screening, Louis-Dreyfus said the black-and-white, 2-D film “took advantage of the art form in an unusual and whatever-the-opposite-of-derivative-is way was very appealing to me. I think it’s very funny and very devastating. I related to it from a personal point of view, having Alzheimer’s in my own family, not once but twice. So, I felt like I understood it and the story needed to be told, and I was honored to be a part of it.”

Cranston observed, “It’s not always the case when you get contacted by people you know and friends saying, ‘We’d like you to be in our movie’ that it’s good.” As the audience laughed, Seth Rogen cut in: “Almost never!” Cranson, with half-joking affirmation from Louis-Dreyfus, continued, “You go, ‘Whoa, how do I get out of this?!’ And then you say something like, ‘You know, I didn’t connect with the character as much as, someone else is going to really, uh …’ But I didn’t have to do any of that. I read the script and I thought, ‘Wow, this is beautiful.'”

Jacobson voices the lead role of Sarah, who at the time the story starts in 1999 is a young artist living in San Francisco, 3,000 miles from her family, exploring a new relationship with a character voiced by Samira Wiley. “I’m playing a real person, who I got to meet. And I read her book, and so that carried a lot of weight for me,” Jacobson Said. A “rarity,” she added, were voice recording sessions that involved multiple cast members performing together. “To get to be in a room doing the voiceover with them is such a different experience. You get to look the other actor in the eye and you get to act and be present. And with a story that’s really important. There’s a lot going on, and it felt so connected, same as the comedic moments. It’s always better when you’re in the room.”

Monday’s event at Regal Union Square was presented by Hilarity for Charity, a not-for-profit founded by the Rogens to support families affected by Alzheimer’s. Joining cast members were Leah Nelson, the film’s director, producer and co-writer; and Levitt, who co-wrote the screenplay. Guests also included former AMC Global Media CEO Josh Sapan, whose company, Sapan Studios, teamed with IFC on the acquisition. (Sapan knows plenty about lengthy film productions, having fostered the Oscar-winning Boyhood during his AMC tenure.) Current AMC Global execs were also out in force, among them CEO Kristin Dolan, Chief Content Officer Dan McDermott; and IFC Entertainment Group chief Scott Shooman.

Michael Jackson’s ‘Thriller’ Scares Up 40th Week at No. 1 on Top R&B/Hip-Hop Albums Chart

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

The set's title track sparks gains for the landmark album after its annual Halloween surge.

For the second time this year, Michael Jackson’s landmark album Thriller glides to the top of the Top R&B/Hip-Hop Albums chart after a mass pop-culture moment.

Unlike the wider spring and summer cycle sparked from the Michael biopic, the latest surge largely centers on one song, the album’s title track, after gains from the Halloween holiday.

Thriller, on the Epic/Legacy record labels, tops the chart dated Nov. 14, rising three spots to crown the list. For its latest coronation, the set earned 55,000 equivalent album units for the Oct. 29 – Nov. 5 tracking week, according to Luminate, an 89% improvement from 29,000 units in the week prior.

Streaming activity contributed 32,000 units of the sum (a figure representing 34.2 million official on-demand streams of the album’s songs), traditional album sums provided 22,000 units and 1,000 units were from track-equivalent album units. (One unit equals the following levels of consumption: one album sale, 10 individual tracks sold from an album, or 1,000 paid/subscription tier or 2,500 ad-supported tier official on-demand audio and video streams for a song on the album.)

With its return, Thriller achieves a 40th week at No. 1 on Top R&B/Hip-Hop Albums, following two weeks at No. 1 in May in the wake of post-Michael mania. The landmark 1982 release, often cited as the best-selling album of all time, held the record for most weeks at No. 1 on the chart from 1984 until 2025, when SZA’s SOS took the title. Thriller narrows the gap with SOS, which remains in first place with 46 weeks on top, having most recently topped the ranking in January.

Here’s a review of the longest leaders on Top R&B/Hip-Hop Albums:

46, SOS, SZA, 2022-2640, Thriller, Michael Jackson, 1983-84; 202629, Please Hammer Don’t Hurt ‘Em, M.C. Hammer, 199026, Just Like the First Time, Freddie Jackson, 1986-8723, Can’t Slow Down, Lionel Richie, 1983-8420, Songs in the Key of Life, Stevie Wonder, 1976-7720, Street Songs, Rick James, 198120, Shoot for the Stars Aim for the Moon, Pop Smoke, 2020-21

Additionally, Thriller’s new frame gives the King of Pop a 101st career week at No. 1 on Top R&B/Hip-Hop Albums, the second-most of all acts since the chart’s launch in 1965. He trails only former Motown labelmates The Temptations, who have posted a record 109 weeks in the penthouse.

Elsewhere, Thriller crowns the Top R&B Albums chart for an 18th week with a 3-1 ascent, while it races 13-2 on the all-genre Billboard 200. On the latter, it achieves its highest rank since 1984, the year it wrapped its 37 weeks atop the list, the most for an album by a single artist.

Gochman Collection Builds a Home for Native Art in Westchester

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The private collection opened its Branchline location in Katonah as a way to bring Native art into public view, though questions linger about its engagement with the local Lenape community.

KATONAH, NY — In 1680, English colonists bought the land that would become Bedford, New York, from a Munsee Lenape sachem named Katonah, meaning “great hill.” Today, his name belongs to a Westchester village that is now home to Branchline, the Gochman Family Collection’s (GFC) new gallery and studio space, which opened to the public on November 6 with Line Break, an exhibition of works drawn primarily from its holdings of contemporary Native American art.

Since its founding in 2021, GFC has collected over 800 works with support from Native and non-Native gallerists, artists, and curators alike, including Creative Director and Curator Rachel Martin (Tlingít), curator and Hyperallergic contributor Shándíín Brown (Diné), and Director Laura Phipps, who also serves as Branchline’s director. Designed by Elizabeth Roberts Architects, the new space occupies two historic storefronts steps from Katonah’s Metro-North station, with free-admission galleries, skylit artist studios, a community ceramics studio, and apartments for invited artists.

Branchline makes a public home for what has essentially been a lending operation: According to GFC, more than 260 of its works have appeared in over 100 exhibitions at institutions including the Metropolitan Museum of Art, the Whitney Museum of American Art, and The Broad, and it charges no loan fees. That model speaks to a known problem with private collecting, explained Joseph Pierce (Cherokee), professor and founding director of the Native American and Indigenous Studies Initiative at Stony Brook University. “Just because you collect something doesn’t mean you’re going to show it,” Pierce told Hyperallergic. “The risk is that it doesn’t circulate.”

This residency at the U-M Stamps School, in partnership with the U-M Museum of Art (UMMA), offers artists a $20,000 honorarium, housing, studio space, and a culminating exhibition in UMMA’s prominent Stenn Gallery.

Sacha Passero is departing IAG after almost 12 years at the talent agency and its predecessor APA. Passero’s contract has come to an end and was not renewed.

The 10 Most Intense Movie Scenes of All Time, Ranked

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Looking over some of the most intense movie scenes of all time is also going to reveal a bunch of movies that themselves can be counted as the most intense of all time. Overlap’s inevitable, but the focus below will be on those individual scenes. Sure, a scene can be long, taking up 10 to 20 percent of a movie’s runtime in some cases, or if it’s suspenseful for a shorter amount of time, maybe it’s an especially harrowing two to three minutes.

Also, talking about specific scenes will mean spoilers can be found below, but not every scene here necessarily takes place near a film’s conclusion. And for one more “also,” these movies have all been out for at least a few years, and most are quite well-known, so you're unlikely to have too much genuinely spoiled here, if you're interested enough in movies overall to read (or skim) a ranking like this.

Since 2027 is less than two months away, at the time of writing, Children of Men might well be an even more stomach-churning watch nowadays than it might've been back in 2006. Most dystopian movies are intense in one way or another, for hopefully obvious reasons, but the bleak future (which isn't a future for much longer) of Children of Men feels especially so.

The first scene is shocking, as are two set pieces that involve a car (one of them in the car, and one involving a car that doesn’t start), yet the final sequence, which sees the surviving characters (not too many) caught up in an all-out war zone, is the most full-on in Children of Men. It contains the most elaborate of the movie’s long takes, and it does an uncomfortably good job at making you feel like you're caught up in all the horror and bloodshed yourself.

There was a time when the shower scene in Psycho might've been genuinely surprising, but nowadays, you know, it’s the shower scene from Psycho. It comes before the halfway point of the movie, and it features the only character who’s been in the entire movie, scene by scene, up until that point. And out of nowhere, she’s murdered in a way that was very shocking by the standards of the early 1960s.

It’s a real shock to the system, or would've been before it became legendary, and so for all that, this scene’s well and truly earned its reputation.

It’s about as famous as thriller movie scenes get, and it played a part in rewriting the rules of what you could and couldn’t do early on in a movie. It’d be shocking enough to have the shower scene happen at the end, but when it happens where it does here, it’s like, “Wow, there’s an hour left, and now literally anything can happen… and actually, what even can happen?” It’s a real shock to the system, or would've been before it became legendary, and so for all that, this scene’s well and truly earned its reputation as an all-time great moment in cinema history.

Alfred Hitchcock got a shout-out here by highlighting Psycho, and then David Fincher, who’s also got a knack for directing suspenseful and effortlessly intense movies, is going to be mentioned shortly. In similar territory is Brian De Palma, and it feels necessary to highlight at least one scene he’s done as being especially intense, so of all the candidates (often finales; see Carrie and Scarface), here’s the big shootout in The Untouchables.

Well, it’s kind of big. The gunfight itself doesn't technically last too long, but The Untouchables spends an agonizingly long time building it all up, and then the use of slow motion during the build-up and when the shooting itself starts also draws the scene out in the best of ways. It’s such an expertly done set piece, and even if De Palma might have some movies that are darker and more consistently tense than The Untouchables, it’s this single scene that best demonstrates his knack for crafting cinematic suspense.

Make no mistake: pretty much the entirety of Zodiac is tense, and then when it’s not tense, it’s more disquieting and despairing. The search for the Zodiac Killer is at the core of the story, unsurprisingly, and most of the tense parts play out when the killer himself is at large, with the more subtly uneasy parts being found in the back half, when the chances of actually finding the killer seem to dissipate further scene by scene.

The terror does come back in full force, though, with the infamous basement scene in Zodiac, which happens closer to the end and involves Robert Graysmith possibly encountering the killer, but the not knowing – and the darkness of the sequence visually – bring the film the closest it gets to outright horror territory. It’s far from the only memorable scene in Zodiac, of course, but it does leave the biggest impact.

The scene you probably remember as being the opening one, even if Saving Private Ryan does start decades after the end of World War II before flashing back, it’s that famous extended sequence depicting the D-Day Landing at Omaha Beach. Guess it is chronologically the first scene that takes place in Saving Private Ryan, and it does have such an impact that it sticking in one’s mind over the actual opening scene is understandable.

There is also a large-scale battle sequence near the end of Saving Private Ryan, and that’s no slouch in terms of showing some truly visceral combat either. However, it's this opening sequence that proves most startling, coming earlier in the film, and it’s also a depiction of an actual battle, so it has that extra sense of dread and terror to it (the final battle is fictionalized, though it does aim to convey World War II-era combat nonetheless).

There are pictures here. You need pictures in an article, apparently. There was an effort made with all the other movies to source relevant pictures (you know, not just showing any moment from Saving Private Ryan or Psycho, but screenshots that convey the scene in question and the relevant intensity). With The Silence of the Lambs, though, the notably tense sequence – or at least the most intense part of that sequence – is memorably very dark, and it’s harder to make things out when it’s not in motion.

It involves Clarice Starling finally confronting Buffalo Bill, and eventually thwarting him, but not until after he switches off all the lights and stalks her around his already creepy house/basement while wearing night-vision goggles. It’s perhaps the part when The Silence of the Lambs most feels like a horror movie, though any big scene with Hannibal Lecter is, quite naturally, also frightening (an honorable mention should go out to his big escape sequence).

You do know this one is coming at all points, and sure, there is even some relief when it happens, just because 127 Hours so perfectly captures – and makes you feel – the utterly nightmarish situation its central character is in throughout. He is hiking without sufficient company and safety equipment, and ends up trapped when a boulder falls on his arm, crushing it and then ultimately not being movable.

So, after several days, he makes the decision to amputate his arm with the very few pieces of equipment he has nearby, and it is what saves his life, sure, but it does also prove nauseating to watch because nothing is shied away from. It’s one of the most painful-to-watch scenes in relatively recent memory, yet it has to be, and there is an understandably great sense of relief alongside the absolute nausea you'll feel witnessing it all.

Jaws is another Steven Spielberg movie that, like Saving Private Ryan, technically begins near or on a beach, and also in a manner that proves surprising and famously intense. That first shark attack scene is monumental, as are a bunch of the sequences that follow throughout the first hour or so of Jaws, though the final act is where the movie obviously crescendos, and if you want to call it the “final showdown,” then that’s it. That’s what’s going here.

You’ve got a whole movie of things not really letting up, and it’s still all quite patiently done, not to mention in a manner that makes being truly invested in that climax inevitable. Jaws is – and forever will be – the best movie about three guys who have to go out to sea and kill a shark, and this extended climax is a big reason why.

Everything in Inglourious Basterds is pretty great, and it’s also a film that has a handful of standout scenes that all manage to feel incredibly tense, but usually in different ways, so there’s no real repetition here. It’s longer than a fair few Quentin Tarantino movies, albeit paced in a way that really makes it all fly by, and all the big scenes help immensely in that regard.

That does also make highlighting one over all the others tough, but here, the one that’s going to be singled out is the tavern sequence. All the other show-stopping scenes are perfect and 10/10-worthy, with this one, like, being an 11/10 scene. Movies don’t get much more nerve-wracking than this, and it’s a shame you can only ever see something for the first time just the once, since being able to experience a scene this perfectly surprising again would be wonderful.

There’s no shark here, but Whiplash does a similar thing to Jaws in terms of having suspense and unease pretty much right from the start, and then having those feelings become more intense as things progress. You're not given a lot of time to breathe, and the dynamic between a dedicated student and a tyrannical instructor gets impossibly uncomfortable, not to mention psychologically harrowing, continually reaching new heights… or lows?

Anyway, that does result in the climax being the biggest and most jaw-dropping sequence in the movie. Whiplash also twists the knife after it’s over, since the movie leaves you hanging in terms of the implications of the eventual moment of triumph, or tragedy. Or it could be a bit of both. It’s hard to know. You'll never know. So you're on the edge of your seat for the final 10 to 15 minutes of Whiplash, and then you continually feel queasy for the next 10 to 15 years after Whiplash came out, whenever you think about it and its ending. That is undoubtedly impressive stuff for a movie’s ending to do.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Capcom still hasn't announced a release date for Monster Hunter Wilds: Ascendance, the Sunbreak-scale DLC that was revealed in February, but it seems like we might be getting a chance to sample the expansion before too long. A recent Steam listing update has provided a strong reason to suspect that there'll be a public beta for Ascendance available ahead of its launch in 2027.

As SteamDB changelogs show, the Steam app entry for Monster Hunter Wilds' 2024 public beta was quietly updated early this morning to change the app's name from "Monster Hunter Wilds Beta test" to "Monster Hunter Wilds: Ascendance Beta test." Similarly, the listing's visual assets were updated to feature key art of a hunter facing an Arateka, one of the new monsters being introduced in the expansion.

And sure enough, while the Wilds beta was removed from player libraries after it ended, it's possible for users who'd previously played it to confirm its listing changes within the Steam app by viewing their own played games from their Steam profile. I just looked. It says Ascendance right there.

As the previous Wilds beta consisted of a repackaged demo build, it's likely that an Ascendance beta would follow suit: I'd expect a similar set of hunts to what in-person attendees were able to get hands-on time with at Tokyo Game Show in September, offering a chance to try out the new Boost Bracer weapon mechanics in Master Rank combat.

If a beta does materialize, it'll hopefully be a smoother experience than what beta hunters got in 2024. The early Wilds playtest was the first time PC players encountered the dreaded origami monsters, a symptom of the severe optimization issues that tanked Wilds' reputation for over a year after launch.

As for when that beta might be available, we can only speculate—but given that Monster Hunter has made prior appearances at Geoff Keighley productions, a public playtest that accompanies a hypothetical Game Awards trailer makes intuitive sense. But I'm just reading the tea leaves here—Capcom hasn't said a word about when it'll offer its next DLC teaser.

Monster Hunter Wilds: Ascendance is set to launch some time in 2027.

MLB rumors: Mets to meet with Tarik Skubal, Rangers' Corey Seager plan, plus Blue Jays, Cubs, Red Sox, more

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

This is an important week on the baseball calendar. First and foremost, it is awards week. Kevin McGonigle and Sal Stewart were named the Rookies of the Year on Monday. The Managers of the Year will be announced Tuesday, Cy Youngs and Relievers of the Year will be announced Wednesday, and finally the MVPs will be announced Thursday.

Also, the GM Meetings are this week in Las Vegas. The GM Meetings are not the Winter Meetings in terms of the sheer volume of rumors and transactions, but they do usually provide some news. There could be more than usual this year given the near certainty of a lockout on Dec. 1, which would also mean there are no Winter Meetings. This may be the only time everyone is in the same room this winter. The three weeks between now and Dec. 1 could be quite active.

We've already ranked the top 50 free agents and the top 25 trade candidates.

With that in mind, here are Tuesday's hot stove rumors.

The Mets have a meeting scheduled with two-time Cy Young winner Tarik Skubal, the clear top free agent avaiable this offseason, later this week, reports the New York Post. Skubal will meet with teams in California, presumably at the offices of agent Scott Boras. Owner Steve Cohen is expected to push for Skubal, according to SNY; president of baseball operations David Stearns has long been reluctant to sign big-money, long-term deals for pitchers, though pitchers of Skubal's caliber are rarely available. The Mets are also seeking first base help and bullpen reinforcements.

Nolan McLean and Christian Scott are the only locks for New York's 2027 rotation at the moment. Justin Hagenman, Sean Manaea, Kodai Senga, Zac Thornton, and Jonah Tong are next in line to make starts. Tylor Megill is expected back from Tommy John surgery early next year as well. The Mets have some depth but clearly need more impact.

The Rangers signed Corey Seager to a 10-year, $325 million deal prior to the 2022 season and it paid off quite handsomely with a World Series title in 2023 (Seager was the World Series MVP). With five years and roughly $155 million left on the deal, however, the Rangers appear ready to trade him.

The Rangers "intend to explore moving Seager at the general managers' meetings," reports The Athletic.

Seager, 32, was stellar from 2023 to 2025 in spite of a litany of injuries, averaging 6.2 WAR per season with a .294 average and 158 OPS+. He actually averaged 8.7 WAR per 162 games. Only the injuries kept him from making multiple serious MVP runs. Last season, though, was a step back. In 102 games, he hit .227/.312/.424 with 20 home runs, 51 RBI and 3.1 WAR.

The injury history keeps getting more extensive, too, as he's dealt with injuries to his thumb, hamstring, hip, other hamstring and back in the last four years.

With a lackluster, at best, shortstop class in free agency, now might be the perfect time for the Rangers to move on from Seager, assuming a deal can be worked out with the years and dollars left on his contract. Texas also has Sebastian Walcott, one of the top prospects in baseball, ready in the wings.

The Blue Jays are among the teams with interest in free agent second baseman Brandon Lowe, according to Sportsnet. Ernie Clement, Toronto's primary second baseman in 2026, crashed hard in the second half and has always fit better as a super utility man who plays regularly at different positions. Josh Smith is the team's other second base option.

Lowe would bring needed power to a team that finished dead last in home runs this past season and had just one player, Kazuma Okamoto (33), clear 15 home runs. Only four Blue Jays hit even 10 homers. Also, Toronto's lineup is mostly right-handed. Lowe would add power and also a lefty bat to provide balance. We ranked him as the 13th-best free agent available.

Adding rotation help is a "top priority," White Sox GM Chris Getz said during an MLB Network Radio interview on Tuesday. "We're going to exhaust the starter market, both free agency -- we'll take a look -- along with trades. That is our top priority," Getz said. He also confirmed that Grant Taylor, the team's postseason closer, will move into the rotation. Hagen Smith will remain in the bullpen.

The White Sox have solid enough rotation depth with Taylor joining Sean Burke, Luis Castillo, Anthony Kay, Davis Martin, David Sandlin, Noah Schultz, and perhaps Shane Smith and Drew Thorpe. There is room for improvement, though, particularly at the top. Chicago could use a bona fide ace, not that one is easy to acquire or abundantly available.

Add the Padres to the list of teams that have shown interest in Skubal, reports MLB Network. San Diego has a major need in the rotation -- Joe Musgrove, who hasn't made a start since the 2024 postseason, and Randy Vásquez are their only rotation locks -- and a new ownership group that seems willing to spend in its first offseason.

Skubal is far and away the offseason's best free agent. He is also a Scott Boras client, and, historically, top Boras clients take the largest contract offer regardless of team situation. That isn't to say the Padres are a bad situation. Not at all. They're a very good team that is in the postseason most years, plus they play in San Diego. Who doesn't love San Diego?

The Padres are also connected to several Japanese players, according to The Athletic. That includes MLB free agents Seiya Suzuki and Shota Imanaga, as well as players coming over from Nippon Professional Baseball, like third baseman Teruaki Sato.

Free agent lefty Adrian Morejon is drawing interest as a starting pitcher, but he prefers to remain in the bullpen, according to The Athletic. Even during the season, there were rumblings that Morejon would like to be given a chance to start in 2027. He came up through the minors as a starter and only remained in the bullpen because he was so dominant in a late-inning role.

We ranked Morejon as the offseason's second-best free agent on the idea that he would move into the rotation. The belief within the game is that he has a chance to be an excellent starter, not just a back-end piece. Either way, Morejon is a very good pitcher, and at 28, he's also one of the youngest free agents available. He should have several peak years remaining.

Although they are disinclined to pursue Tarik Skubal, the Cubs hope to add three established starting pitchers this offseason, reports The Athletic. Of the nine pitchers to make at least nine starts for Chicago in 2027, seven are free agents. Only Ben Brown, who recently had neck surgery (per ESPN), and Edward Cabrera, who had a 5.96 ERA and was demoted to the bullpen, remain.

Left-hander Justin Steele is expected back from UCL surgery next year, though he suffered a setback this summer and wasn't able to return as hoped in September. Others like Javier Assad and Jordan Wicks are depth options more than full-time rotation pieces. This free agent class offers plenty of second- and third-tier starters. That's where the Cubs are expected to shop.

Unlike last offseason, the Mets are not interested in a major overhaul despite their disappointing year, reports MLB.com. The Mets went 74-88 and finished in last place in 2026. The only silver lining to the disappointing season was the ability to give a long runway to rookies like Carson Benge and Nolan McLean, both of whom finished in the top four of the Rookie of the Year voting.

The Mets went 83-79 in 2025 and missed the postseason on the final day of the season. They made significant changes last winter, most notably jettisoning stalwarts Pete Alonso and Brandon Nimmo, then had a worse season in 2026. In September, president of baseball operations David Stearns admitted that much turnover created an "uncapped downside scenario," which is executive-speak for "there was too much risk."

Not surprisingly, the Braves are uninterested in moving former National League MVP Ronald Acuña Jr., according to MLB Network. It does feel like Atlanta's offense has gone stale and that they could move a core piece, but it won't be Acuña, nor is it likely to be Drake Baldwin, Michael Harris II, or Matt Olson. Ozzie Albies and Austin Riley are the more obvious players to move. Albies is entering the final season of his wildly team-friendly contract. Riley has six years remaining on his deal, which figures to make a move difficult.

On Monday, Red Sox chief baseball officer Craig Breslow declined to commit to Trevor Story as his starting shortstop next season. "We still have a ton of confidence in Trevor, but any decisions like that are going to take place between Trevor and me far before I speak to anything publicly," Breslow told reporters, including the Boston Globe. Boston has one of the top prospects in the minors in shortstop Franklin Arias, who is very likely to make his debut early next year. Arias could push Story into a utility role or off the team entirely.

A viral TikTok trend is turning ordinary photos into something unexpectedly emotional

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Looking back through old photographs often happens in a family living room, turning the pages of a loved one’s photo albums. But on TikTok, the nostalgic ritual is its own trend, and it’s set to the tune of Pink Floyd.

In a new trend on TikTok, users are showcasing their life progress by looking back to their childhood, comparing their baby photos to recent ones. While the captions vary across videos, it’s the music and lyrics that brings all of them together: Pink Floyd’s Time.

The trend uses the photo-carousel feature on TikTok as a template: the first photo is of the user at a young age, with the next one being a recent image. The first photograph has the beginning of one of the song’s lines typed on it, “The sun is the same, in a relative way,” followed by the “but you are older,” written in the recent photo.

For many users, the trend is a way to show that, despite years passed, key attributes of the user remain the same. Take content creator Eugenia McQuarrie, who posted a photo of herself at a beauty salon, with rollers in her hair. The photo is followed by a present day image of McQuarrie, wearing hair rollers as an adult.

A user replied saying, “diva since day one.”

Another user had a similar take on the trend, first posting a photo of himself as a kid, wearing a firefighter toy hat, cutting to his grown up version wearing a real-life firefighter uniform.

The trend has quickly gained momentum on the platform, with over 8.8 million views in the span of weeks, according to trend-tracking agency Masterhooks.

Part of the appeal is the trend’s versatility, allowing users to make it their own, turning to videos and edits, keeping only the soundtrack.

Sometimes it was the small mannerisms that remained unchanged by time, which users wanted to highlight. Like one user who first shows a video of her dancing as a kid in her living room, then cuts to her dancing at a party as an adult.

“Still the same vibe, how cute,” one user commented.

For others, nostalgia didn’t reach as far back as most. Ballet content creator Katelyn Power, for instance, posted a video of her dancing ballet four years earlier, cut to her most recent performance of the same dance.

A user commented, “Pink Floyd and ballet? I am in heaven.”

Henry Cavill's The Witcher Replacement Has One Major Advantage Over Geralt Of Rivia

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

After starring on Netflix's The Witcher for three seasons, Henry Cavill's big role in an upcoming movie is the perfect next step in the actor's career, with one major advantage over his previous fantasy part. Before Liam Hemsworth took over the role of Geralt in season 4, Cavill had become synonymous with the character, making his exit so upsetting.

The world of The Witcher is bigger than any one cast member. The Witcher: Sirens of the Deep had a different Geralt, voiced by Doug Cockle, and built on a legacy that lasted even longer than Cavill had in the part. Cockle previously voiced Geralt of Rivia in all three The Witcher video games, helping to craft the character onscreen.

Considering that the games themselves informed much of the look and feel of the series, and were themselves drawn from The Witcher novels, that world is actually large enough to last without Henry Cavill. And the actor's talents have now been pointed toward a perfect role, with a character who has one major advantage over Geralt of Rivia.

Cavill's career in Hollywood is sure to be long and full of fantastic roles, but this upcoming project could become a defining film for the star.

After having played such iconic roles as Superman and Sherlock Holmes, his new take on another popular and beloved part could be the perfect chance for Cavill to do something truly unique, thanks to an incredible ability.

Henry Cavill will star in the upcoming remake of Highlander, where his lead character, Connor MacLeod, will have a power that no other characters in Cavill's past have. Connor MacLeod is an Immortal and serves as the center of the story as the titular Highlander, who is set to one day fight for The Prize.

While there is an inherent tension to Connor's existence, as captured by the original 1986 Highlander film's popular quote, "There can be only one", this is still an incredible power.

Born in the 16th century in Scotland, Connor has spent centuries exploring the Earth and training as a swordsman in order to one day be able to fight to become the last of his kind.

Connor has possessed some powers in addition to his immortality. During the Quickening, when an Immortal is killed, generally by another Immortal, a surge of energy escapes their body, allowing the victor to absorb the strength and memories of their fallen foe. This adds to a powerset that also includes enhanced regeneration.

Still, even with all of Connor's fantastic abilities, the character is important to compare against the roles that Cavill has played in the past. And while Geralt of Rivia might not be immortal, he does possess some fantastic skills that might make him even stronger than Connor could ever hope to be.

Geralt of Rivia actually shares some similarities in his powers with Connor MacLeod. While not hampered by a connection to a race of Immortals, which is itself an asset to Geralt's mortality, The Witcher's protagonist also has the benefit of slow aging, as well as tremendous skills in regeneration and healing.

Geralt, another trained swordsman, has greater speed, skill, and reflexes than Connor has generally been depicted as having. A fantastic tracker with elevated senses, Geralt has also been mutated in several ways that contribute to his incredibly enhanced physical powers of movement, fighting, and more.

If Geralt of Rivia and Connor MacLeod ever met in battle, it is hard to say exactly who would emerge triumphant, and much of that will depend on how the upcoming film depicts Connor. However, the more battle-hardened and practiced Geralt of Rivia would likely have at least a small advantage over his Highlander foe.

Regardless, both characters are excellent parts for Henry Cavill. And while The Witcher will continue to live on without him in some form, even after the Netflix series ends, this new and thrilling story of a rebooted Highlander is only about to get started.

'Chimp Crazy' Tonka is Safe After Florida Floods

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

The Florida sanctuary where beloved chimpanzee Tonka lives has been rocked by floods ... but the "Chimp Crazy" star is safe and sound.

Dan Mathews, Director of Events and Special Projects for Save the Chimps, tells TMZ … Save the Chimps got 19 inches of rain in 12 hours.

Mathews tells us ... all the chimps were brought inside except one named Kaleb. He says they weren’t able to get to him during the flooding and have been giving him items via drone.

We're told Tonka rode out the storm alongside 15 other chimps in the care house, and had a blast hanging out with fellow chimp, Kippy.

While the chimps are all doing just fine in the wake of the wild weather, Mathews tells TMZ the sanctuary's got a lot of damage.

He says Save the Chimps hasn't seen such intense flooding for the 25 years it's been in Fort Pierce.

The sanctuary's still assessing the damage, but Mathews estimates they're looking at over $100K in damage ... and they're turning to the public for financial help.

He says they need to re-surface the road around the property, fill in sinkholes, repair their buggy, replace their golf carts, repair roofs, re-landscape several parts of the refuge, and more.

He says the rain stopped on Tuesday and the water's gone down ... but it still hasn't gone away.

Why Joshua Jackson Was Only Dawson's Creek Star Missing From Memoir

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

In Kerr Smith’s new memoir I Don’t Want to Wait: My Life on Dawson’s Creek, Joshua Jackson was not interviewed like this fellow cast—and Smith shared why in an exclusive E! News interview.

Kerr Smith didn’t want to wait to tell his Dawson’s Creek story.

And so, fans of The WB series might notice that while Katie Holmes, Michelle Williams and James Van Der Beek sat for interviews for his new memoir I Don’t Want to Wait: My Life on Dawson’s Creek, Joshua Jackson did not return to Capeside—and that wasn’t Smith’s decision.

“All I want to say is everybody makes a choice about whether or not they want to be part of the project or not,” Smith told E! News in an exclusive interview. “Some did, some didn't.”

And Smith appeared to nod to Jackson’s hesitation to participate in a parenthetical within the book itself, which hit shelves today, Nov. 10, while recounting that scene between Holmes’ Joey and Jackson’s Pacey in the series finale.

While pushing back on Holmes’ admission that she didn’t love her performance, Smith revealed Jackson wasn’t available to share his perspective.

“Allow me to weigh in with my expert analysis. (Yep, I’m now an expert. I mean, have you not heard? I wrote the book on this stuff.),” Smith wrote. “With all due respect, you’re wrong. You positively crushed this scene. Both of you. Right, Josh? Josh? (I’m sure he’ll get back to me any second now.)”

E! News reached out to Jackson’s rep for comment but hasn’t heard back.

But while Smith and Jackson didn’t go down memory lane, or rather creek, together, the Final Destination alum emphasized what it meant to him to revisit series with the rest of the cast and crew.

“I wanted to get everybody else’s memory on it too,” Smith told E!. “I did over two dozen interviews for the book so I wanted to get everybody’s experiences and put it in one place to understand what a great show this is.”

When it came to his conversations with Holmes, the 54-year-old mused that it was easy for them to simply snap right back into the late 1990s, adding, “It’s like we never even lost a step.”

“You make those bonds back then and you just kind of hang on to them,” he added. “It doesn’t matter how many years have gone by.”

And, of course, it was especially sentimental for Smith to get to reminisce with Van Der Beek before his death earlier this year after a three-year battle with colorectal cancer.

“When I did interview James, he was already pretty sick,” Smith noted. “But he was kind enough to give me a few hours and we had an amazing discussion.”

In addition to being a reflection on Dawson’s Creek as a whole, Smith also included a chapter for former cast and crew to share their memories with Van Der Beek following his death.

“I wanted to give everybody that knew him back then a chance to say anything at all if they wanted to,” Smith shared. “I just thought it was really nice. I think it’s something that he would’ve been OK with.”

As he put it, “I think it’s a good way to end the story.”

You can read the entire fantastic, Stephen Spielberg-film worthy story in Smith’s new memoir, and keep reading for even more secrets into the beloved teen drama…

While Fox was first to pick up Dawson's Creek, it subsequently dumped the teen drama.

"I was told they were struggling with Party of Five and they didn't need another one," series creator Kevin Williamson said at the 2015 ATX TV Festival.

But that paved the way for The WB to pick it up two years later.

While Paula Cole's "I Don't Want to Wait" became the iconic Dawson's Creek theme song, producers originally wanted Alanis Morissette's "Hand in My Pocket."

Alas, they couldn't land the rights, what a jagged little pill.

Dawson. Pacey. Joey. Not names you hear every day. So how did Williamson come up with them?

"Dawson came from a real place called Dawson's Creek where we all hung out as kids and partied," he told The Hollywood Reporter in 2018, while Pacey "came from a friend of a friend named Pacey and I'd never met anyone with that name and thought it was a cool name."

And for the show's heroine, Williamson "wanted Josephine as a very girly name that could easily turn into a tomboy name like Joey."

Remember Dawson's infamous "walk the dog" line in the pilot?

Williamson inserted the euphemism because the network would not let them say the word "masturbate" in 1998.

Before Katie Holmes was cast in the role that would make her America's sweetheart-next-door, Blair was this close to landing the role.

"Joe was written to be a tomboy and everyone was coming in being very much a tomboy. We were very close to going with Selma Blair, who was amazing," Williamson told THR. "She read it very tough, with a lot of heart."

However, once he saw Holmes on tape, with "those two big eyes," Williamson knew they had their Joey.

Williamson liked Joshua Jackson so much, he didn't care what role the Mighty Ducks alum played.

"I fell in love with Josh Jackson because he could read any role, Dawson or Pacey," he told THR. "But something wasn't complete and that's when the network said they didn't see Josh as Dawson, and rightfully so. So, I went, 'OK, he's Pacey,' because I knew I wanted him in the show no matter what."

Charlie Hunnam Auditioned, Won Role of Husband

An 18-year-old Charlie Hunnam read for the WB drama in 1999 and, while he didn't land the part, he did meet Katherine Towne at his audition.

They tied the knot in Las Vegas three weeks later and divorced in 2002, "three terrible, painful, expensive years later," as Hunnam put it to the Associated Press in 2017.

Dawson's Creek became so popular that American Eagle inked a deal in 2000 to outfit the actors.

The characters wore mostly AE clothes throughout season three, and the cast appeared in ad campaigns and promos for the clothing line, helping drive the company's net income above $105 million in 2001.

When James Van Der Beek was first cast, the studio wanted him to change his hair.

"We found an ad for The Devil's Own, the movie with Brad Pitt," he recalled to The Daily Beast in 2012. "They said: 'What about Brad Pitt's haircut?' That's how I got my season one haircut."

Holmes dated Jackson early in the show's run, before Pacey and Joey realized they opposite-of-hated each other.

"I'm just going to say that I met somebody last year," Holmes told Rolling Stone in 1998, not denying that the somebody was Jackson. "I fell in love, I had my first love, and it was something so incredible and indescribable that I will treasure it always. And that I feel so fortunate because he's now one of my best friends. It's weird, it's almost like a Dawson-and-Joey type thing now."

Plus one more nugget: "He's been in the business so long," she added, "and he's really helped me. I respect him as a friend and a professional."

When Kerr Smith first signed on to join the show as Joey's new boyfriend, he had no idea the character would eventually come out and be part of the first-ever gay kiss on TV.

"I always knew I wanted Jack to come out of the closet, but I didn't even tell [Smith]," Williamson said at the ATX TV Festival. "Let the audience love him, then let's have him come out of the closet and have Joey have to deal with that—and then that would eventually drive her back to [Dawson]."

More poignantly, Williamson has said that every character on the show inherited traits from him, including Jack.

“Every single character has a trait of me in them,” he told Entertainment Weekly in 2018. “I had just, in my 20s, gone through the coming-out process and had told my parents I was gay. I had taken that whole journey, and I wanted a character on the show to represent that journey and to represent that side of me."

Jack's coming out episode, cowritten by Williamson and producer Greg Berlanti, was inspired by true events.

"A friend of mine had a story that I brought in that he got outed accidentally," Berlanti told EW, "because he wrote a love poem that it was so clear to everyone else that it was about a guy.”

Michelle Williams and Busy Philipps' best-friendship, which first blossomed on Dawson's Creek, is still going strong to this day.

“I’m so in love with her,” Williams told People of her bestie at a 2016 screening of Manchester by the Sea, one of many red carpet date nights for the besties. “She’s proof that the love of your life does not have to be a man! That’s the love of my life right there.”

Philipps is also godmother to Williams' daughter Matilda Ledger, who was born in 2005.

Dawson finally losing his virginity was such a big show moment that Van Der Beek brought a very special gift to set the day the scene was filmed.

"I remember bringing champagne for the crew—Dawson finally lost his virginity!" he told The Daily Beast.

Before starring on One Tree Hill, Chad Michael Murray had an arc on Dawson's Creek playing Charlie, a love interest for Jen and Joey.

But while Charlie got around, Murray didn't seem to make a lot of friends during his first stint in North Carolinav (OTH later filmed there).

During a Paley panel in 2009, Philipps called him "a douche," later saying, "Don't worry. I'm not real worried about burning bridges with CMM."

Van Der Beek added, "He's actually come a long way." Post-event, Philipps tweeted, "Just finished the dawson's creek panel. Maybe I was too harsh on chadM2… Nah."

Murray, meanwhile, hasn't stirred the pot, saying in 2015 that he learned a lot from the experience.

“I had the opportunity to learn from people who had been doing it for a couple of years longer than I was when I walked into the Dawson’s cast,” he told Cosmpolitan.com in 2015. “You know, Michelle and James and Katie and Josh—they really kind of guided me, because they’d already been around for five, six years, maybe longer. And if they had the time, they were showing me the ropes. I had no idea what lighting was, I had no idea about a lot of the technical aspects of what we do, and so I’m grateful for that experience.”

Just a sampling of the famous faces, ranging from the already familiar to future huge stars, who appeared on Dawson's Creek over the years: Seth Rogen, Jane Lynch, Julie Bowen, Scott Foley, Jensen Ackles, Hilarie Burton, Rachel Leigh Cook, Ali Larter and Oliver Hudson.

Young Americans was a short-lived spinoff centering on Pacey's friend Will Krudski (Rodney Scott) and his boarding school classmates at Rawley Academy.

Also starring Ian Somerhalder and Kate Bosworth, it was canceled after just one season.

The Show Almost Ended With Joey Picking Dawson

The show's main love triangle officially disassembled when Joey chose Pacey in the 2003 series finale.

But it almost didn't turn out that way, as Williamson first envisioned Dawson getting the girl. But halfway through writing the finale, Williamson realized that a happy ending for that couple wasn't "what the show was set to be."

"I wanted it to be a twist on the teen genre but also wanted it to be surprising, honest and real and say something about soul mates and what soul mates can be," he explained at the ATX TV Festival. "That's why we did it that way. When you left the show in that last moment, they're a family and everyone got what they wanted. There was fulfillment and they were all happy."

At the last minute, he changed his mind and the rest is TV history. But Williamson admitted his mom, who played for Team Dawson, was not happy.

In the finale, which jumped ahead five years, the group had to deal with the devastating loss of Jen.

"Dealing with the death of one of their own was the final thing that thrust them into adulthood forever. Dawson's Creek was a coming-of-age story and that was the idea behind that ending," Williamson explained of the decision. "That's why we killed Jen, because I wanted them to deal with a death of one their own as that final lesson."

Some of the executive producers who get their start on Dawson's?

Julie Plec (who went on to gift us with The Vampire Diaries and The Originals), Berlanti (Everwood, Arrow, The Flash, Riverdale...the list goes on and on, and that doesn't even include his movies), Rob Thomas (Veronica Mars and iZombie), Jenny Bicks (Sex and the City), Anna Fricke (Being Human), Dana Baratta (Jessica Jones) and many more.
